# Remus master roadmap

**The single source of truth for priorities, dependencies, implementation status,
and remaining work.** Start here, choose one bounded item, and record the PR's
evidence for its row as a [fragment](roadmap-evidence/README.md). Specifications define scope and acceptance; audits retain
source-pinned evidence. Neither maintains a competing work queue.

**Priority review:** 2026-09-21 against `main` at
`d7f78cb91e5a8b5baf589f52d47c4d1463991694`. This review updates sequencing and
acceptance gates, not implementation completion or measured kernel rankings.
The full-register reconciliation below remains the 2026-09-13 snapshot at
`4916babcda39b75d6e71deb2bcb69307aa900c95`.
Open PRs below are a dated snapshot, not completion evidence or permanent ownership.
This documentation review preserves existing qualification limits; it does not
re-run geometry suites or certify the current scheduled jobs.
**Consumer overlay 2026-09-26:** OpenZCAD's reference-CAD comparison
([record](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/blob/e0b982a8111e17636a9de6e4e8789bf1d9aadee4/docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md))
added scope notes to 7.1, 7.2, 7.4, 7.5, 4.3, 6.1 and 6.6, bridge rows B72–B75,
and two decision rows. It changes no state and no evidence.

**File-interoperability overlay 2026-09-29:** OpenZCAD
[PR #486](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/486) adds I06–I20.

### How to read status and IDs

`Pending` / `Open` means the registered scope has no recorded completion;
`Partial` identifies a landed subset and names the remainder. `Implemented`,
`Merged`, `Complete` and `Done` retain their original bounded qualification
claims: read the limitations in the same row. None grants capability promotion
or competitive parity beyond the evidence. An open PR is **in flight**, never
Done. `Proposed` performance packages require baseline and dependency review;
it does not mean no relevant implementation already exists.

IDs stay compatible with existing issues and PRs. Use **P-Class 2.5** for a
capability issue, **B4** for a bridge row, **EXIT-B4** for the former P-Class
exit benchmark B4, and **PERF-B04** for performance package B04. The generated
performance CSV/JSON retain their original short IDs and schema. Only this
page owns implementation state; those files are generated work-spec exports.
The [capability matrix](capability-matrix.md) still governs qualification and
the [stability matrix](../production-readiness/stability-matrix.md) governs
public labels. Consolidating planning does not change either authority.

## Current priorities

Prioritize reliably supported workflows: consistent operation contracts, verified
source/package behavior, authoritative boundaries, and independent qualification
before broader operation coverage. Existing completed subsets remain completed;
the follow-up gates below do not reopen them without a reproduced regression.
The lanes permit independent work after a live file-overlap check; they are not
permission to launch several changes in one session. These are dependency gates,
not engineer-week estimates or calendar commitments.

| Priority / lane | Next bounded work and owner | Completion boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 — Exactness and disclosure | Preserve [B21](#b21)'s landed exact-only Booleans; reconcile stale comments and audit remaining public entry points with [O4.7](#o-o4-7), [B1](#b1), and P-Class 3.6. | Native facade, direct WASM, and batch agree on exact success, disclosed approximation, repair, and typed refusal. Approximation requires an explicit permissive call; no bare-handle success may hide fallback. |
| 1 — Distributed behavior | [O1.5](#o-o1-5), O4.2a/b and O3.1a: qualify the installed package alongside source contracts. | Record source SHA, artifact hashes and build configuration; run positive, refusal, rollback and disclosure witnesses through native and npm-installed WASM. A source test or package-refresh PR alone is insufficient. |
| 2 — Boundary and result authority | P-Class 2.0 follow-up validation audit, 2.6 and M3; [B20](#b20) property-option follow-up; [B18](#b18) result-entity coverage. | Trace concrete public call paths before changing behavior. Check full edge trims and per-use pcurves, dimensional tolerances, effective measurement options, and complete result attribution. Preserve existing migration and measurement qualification. |
| 3 — Independent qualification baseline | [B26](#b26), B19, B6, B10 and O1.2a–f; extend the existing O1.2d schema, O1.5 matrix and gauntlet. | A pinned representative suite reports correct exact/empty results, disclosed approximation, refusal, incorrect success, timeout, crash and resource failure separately. Independent geometry checks and explicit skipped cells accompany coverage and timing. |
| 4 — Bounded geometry expansion | P-Class 2.4/2.5/2.7 and B7/B8 as prerequisites; then named M5 curved-blend and 5.7b offset families. | Each slice declares supported geometry and boundary refusals, passes the qualification gates below, and updates only its own supported domain. B4's planar completion and 5.7's bounded collapse case remain credited. Serialize shared GFA/splitter work. |
| 5 — Consumer and performance follow-through | B16 adapter adoption, B28 / PERF-N04, O3.1a baseline extensions and one profile-selected cost. | Retire a named consumer heuristic or demonstrate a measured improvement on equivalent-quality successful workloads. Separate refusal rate, geometry validity, native/WASM behavior and latency; preserve numerical and rollback contracts. |
| 6 — Broader platform scope | O4.4/O4.6, O5.1/O5.4 assembly exchange, B29 transactions and O3.2 prepared queries. | Follow existing dependencies. Assembly/PMI fidelity, caching, concurrency and general imported-body editing need their own evidence; geometry-only STEP success does not close them. |

### Qualification gates for the priority lanes

These gates refine the linked owners' remaining work; they are not a second
implementation register. A milestone names one operation family, its supported
inputs, its numerical/error contract and the evidence needed for promotion.

| Owner / milestone | Required acceptance evidence |
| --- | --- |
| B21 / O4.7 / B1 — trustworthy outcomes | Inventory public native, direct and batch entry points, including options/evolution wrappers. Retain B21's existing refusal/rollback tests; add witnesses only for uncovered paths. Quality, repair actions and verification status must be observable where applicable. An exact-only request cannot publish a faceted fallback. Correct documentation to match actual dispatch. |
| O1.5 / O4.2a/b — source-to-package parity | Build generated packages from the recorded source; test the installed tarball, not only a native binding helper. Pin exact-result, approximation-opt-in, typed-refusal, cancellation and transactional-state cases. Record target/toolchain/package identity and artifact hashes. Reuse the existing parity harness; retain its known approximation-partition differences as separately reported cells. |
| P-Class 2.0 / 2.6 / M3 — boundary consistency | Audit residual legacy validators and compatibility mutation paths against authoritative trims/coedges. Verify 3D edge versus each face-use pcurve over the full trim, periodic seams and parameter remapping; exercise rotation, translation, and both scaled tolerances and fixed physical tolerances. Keep the landed reader/mutation ratchets. Never widen global tolerances to clear a witness. |
| B20 follow-up / B18 — measurement and history | Determine which public paths expose `adaptive_eps` and `max_depth`; make advertised controls effective or explicitly unsupported with compatibility documentation. Check convergence against independent analytic or adaptive reference values, not another wrapper over the same integrator. *(Measurement half met 2026-09-25; see [B20](#b20).)* Compare attributed entities against the actual result-entity set; test deliberately omitted records, split/merge events and typed unresolved outcomes. Extend face/edge/vertex coverage one family at a time. |
| B26 / B19 / B6 / B10 / O1.2 — qualified workload baseline | Pin fixture rights/hashes, source/build/configuration, seeds and family denominators. Include application-representative construction chains, STEP round trips, seams, tangencies, cavities and scale/placement variants. Track incorrect accepted results separately from useful-operation coverage; refusals and skipped/expensive checks remain visible. Use independent geometry/occupancy/property checks alongside topology and mesh checks. Time the same correct, equivalent-quality successes on each kernel; report native and WASM separately. |
| M5 / 6.1 / 7.4 — curved blends | Select one named support/contact/corner family. Prove neighboring-face relimitation, shared edges and pcurves, orientation, declared continuity and geometric error, complete construction history, and transactional refusal beyond the family. A lower-level untrimmed return first needs a public-operation reproduction; it is not by itself evidence of an accepted invalid solid. |
| P-Class 5.7b — cavity and global offset interference | Separate joint semantics and both offset signs. Include two expanding cavities that collide while their extents remain inside the outer extent, thin-wall collapse and nonlocal intersections. Independently verify material regions and shell separation, or require a typed transactional refusal. Bounding-box containment and a watertight preview are insufficient; retain 5.7's existing bounded qualification. |

### Evidence triage (2026-09-21)

Source observations below were checked at the priority-review SHA. They are
investigation inputs, not newly reproduced runtime failures or reopened completed
milestones. Record **evidence class, impact, confidence, public-path witness and
owner** before promoting an observation to a defect. Distinguish implementation
behavior, reproduced incorrect success, missing qualification and vendor claims.

- **B21 remains Done.** The comparison's silent-fallback claim follows a stale
  `booleanWithQuality` comment. Plain `fuse`/`cut`/`intersect` dispatch to
  `boolean()` and `exact_only_context()`; this is also true at the comparison's
  `76dbffd` snapshot. The follow-up is documentation consistency and contract/
  package verification, not reimplementing the already-landed default.
- **P-Class 2.0 migration remains credited.** Residual NURBS checks in
  `crates/check/src/validate/edge.rs` use the carrier domain and compare a
  parameter span with linear tolerance. Trace their public validation impact
  before claiming that strict validation accepts invalid geometry. Compatibility
  facades alone do not prove the completed ownership migration failed.
- **B20's bounded measurement completion remains credited.** The property
  wrappers in `crates/check/src/properties/mod.rs` forward quadrature order but
  not the advertised adaptive controls. Audit exposed options and independent
  error evidence; do not infer that all existing measurements are wrong.
  *Resolved 2026-09-25:* at this SHA the check wrappers already forwarded all
  three controls; the gaps were refusal on every trimmed/freeform face and no
  controls on the operations, facade and WASM paths. See the [B20](#b20) row.
- **B18 owns the history follow-up.** `EvolutionMap::is_complete()` checks the
  unresolved bucket, not a census of result entities. Test actual producers and
  consumers before attributing a lost persistent selection to this helper.
- **M5 and 5.7b retain their named limits.** Nonplanar blend trimming and global
  cavity interference need the witnesses above. The fixed NURBS STEP carrier
  regression stays fixed; an inconsistent-input refusal is not automatically an
  import defect. Preserve the [STEP audit](step-roundtrip-audit-2026-09-18.md)'s
  distinction between geometry, measurement and explicit rejection.

Representation quality, geometric error and topology validity are separate
claims: retaining analytic/NURBS B-Rep does not make numerical intersection or
fitting symbolically exact. Cross-kernel rankings require the same measured
workloads; vendor documentation and source inspection establish different kinds
of evidence. This roadmap adds no competitive ranking or schedule estimate.

### Correctness integration (2026-09-13)

This replacement combines the four inspected heads above on current main and
preserves the original branches. The findings below are historical inputs;
regressions in this integration now cover each reported failure. #422 has merged.

- B26 native: the unchanged-stock Fuse plus permitted sibling refusals is rejected;
  box, plug, cavity, scale and generated-box closed forms run independently.
- B26 fuzz: successful non-finite volumes panic; refused roots still compare
  nudge outcomes; closed curves validate trim authority and use geometric keys.
  Run the injected tests with `cargo test --manifest-path fuzz/Cargo.toml --bin boolean_tree`.
- P-Class 2.5: the closed tube stays closed; open near-full tubes preserve their
  physical gap across rotations and parameter-axis orders. General curved
  trimmed-face booleans remain unqualified.
- B28: probes observe the same integral used by the orientation verdict, with
  no second integration. Enabled, skipped and cavity-shell cases are covered;
  this is a work census, not an enforced interactive latency budget.

| Owner / reviewed head | Recorded finding / remaining gate |

| B26 / #417 `1573b516` | Successful non-finite measurements were classified as refusal; a base-refused root skipped nudge comparison; coincident endpoints bypassed closed-curve domain authority. Retain all three injected witnesses before crediting the fuzz slice. |
| P-Class 2.5 / #416 `3a301636` | Re-review still reproduced endpoint gap zero for the open near-full tube. The added gap tolerance of 0.05 exceeds the physical gap of about 0.02 and accepts closure; require a genuinely discriminating regression and carrier continuation evidence. |
| B28 / #413 `e401170d` | Probes re-integrated shells after validation rather than observing actual work. Collect actual integration counters, cover skipped orientation, and distinguish work identity from an enforced latency budget. |
| B24 / #422 predecessor | Exhaustive volume matches and a 166-arm ratchet were proposed; other dense-file conversions and immutable workflow-source/caller wiring remain part of the parent acceptance. |
| B26 / #410 `865f0c1f` | Re-review still reproduced refusal masking: an unchanged stock returned as a successful fuse passes topology checks, then another operation’s refusal skips the known-volume oracle. Apply every independent oracle before the refusal return and retain the injected witness. |
| B28 / #418 `937cae61` | The reviewer reported 33,852 derivative vectors matching bit-for-bit. This comparison belongs to that earlier head and is not the full performance or current-head qualification gate. |

### Proof-gate refresh (2026-09-16)

All four workflows have active `schedule` triggers in `.github/workflows`
(`fuzz.yml`, `mutants.yml`, `gauntlet.yml`, `osv-scan.yml`) and none is
disabled — `gh api repos/esaueng/remus/actions/workflows` reports every one
`active`, and each of Fuzz Smoke and Mutation Testing shows an unbroken weekly
schedule history back through August. The premise that Fuzz Smoke and Mutation
Testing had "no scheduled run in the last 100 runs" was a window artefact:
the last-100 list only reaches back to 2026-09-14, while both are weekly
Sunday jobs, so at most one scheduled run each can appear in it. No trigger
was re-enabled or fixed because none was broken. Caller pins
(`fuzz.yml` → fleet `055b7fc0`, `mutants.yml` → fleet `dbb22761`,
`gauntlet.yml` → fleet `6da8b6a0`) are all reachable commits.

Each workflow was run once by dispatch on 2026-09-16 and read back:

| Job | Verdict | Run |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Corpus Gauntlet (schedule, 2026-09-16) | success | [`35074386370`](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/actions/runs/35074386370) |
| OSV Scan (schedule, 2026-09-14) | success | [`34839089074`](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/actions/runs/34839089074) |
| Mutation Testing (dispatch, 2026-09-16) | success — 0 missed, 0 timed-out on the week's `--in-diff` scope | [`35059075161`](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/actions/runs/35059075161) |
| Fuzz Smoke (dispatch, 2026-09-16) | **failure** — `modifier_ops` volume oracle | [`35059073599`](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/actions/runs/35059073599) |

The fuzz failure is the same defect class as the 2026-09-13 scheduled failure
([`34747689405`](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/actions/runs/34747689405),
also `modifier_ops`): the `assert_measurements_agree` oracle reports
`mass_properties` ~3–5% above `solid_volume` on a torus-bored box after a
small fillet. Both crash inputs were replayed natively and both measurements
printed before believing the message: the Gauss route (~36.41) integrates the
true boundary and agrees with the converged closed mesh (~36.41), while the
tessellated route (~35.27) under-reads. Root: the bored body routes to
`volume_from_direct_face_tessellation`, whose exact-analytic gate measured
NURBS-trimmed planes by chord while those faces' own tessellation walks the
raw knot domain instead of the edge trim. Fix: `planar_face_signed_volume`
now reports an `exact_boundary` bit, the exact gate declines chorded planes,
and the direct fallback re-routes such bodies to the closed whole-solid mesh;
regression `crates/operations/tests/regress_volume_nurbs_trimmed_planes.rs`
fails before and passes after. See
[#463](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/463) (open at this writing; its
CI `Test` failure is the pre-existing `revolve_orientation_holds_across_scales`
scale assertion, which fails identically on unmodified `main` and passes at
the current head — not a regression from the fix).
Package #408 (2.130.15, source `4e6499fc`) predates #409/#411/#418.
Closed candidates #419 and #425 predate newer integrated source; rebuild the
committed distribution from the final source before claiming package parity.

### Consumer overlay (2026-09-26)

OpenZCAD compared itself against a mainstream direct-modeling CAD's full manual
([record](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/blob/e0b982a8111e17636a9de6e4e8789bf1d9aadee4/docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md)) and revised its
roadmap. Its new product rows that need kernel work are mapped here so each has
one owner; rows it can build on the current pin are left to the consumer. The
mapping is scope only — no state, evidence or priority changed.

| OpenZCAD row | Needs from the kernel | Owner here |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M04 sweep depth (twist, scale law, profile position, orientation, mitre/round corners) and loft depth (guide curves, vertex correspondence, periodic, G1/G2 end tangency) | `sweepWithOptions` carries contact mode, corner mode, a scale law and segments, unqualified; the native auxiliary-spine option is not bound there (`guidedSweep` is separate), and twist is absent. Loft options carry only `ruled`, `startPoint` and `endPoint`. | [7.1](#p-7-1), [7.2](#p-7-2) |
| M03 blend slices (chord-width sizing, G2 cross-section, tangent-edge chain selection, corner and overflow policy) | Sizing and continuity variants are unowned; setbacks and cliff detection are [5.4](#p-5-4)/[5.5](#p-5-5). | [B72](#b72) |
| M07 Replace Face (extend or trim faces to a target face) | Replace-surface re-limitation and the surface extension it needs. | [6.1](#p-6-1), [7.4](#p-7-4) |
| M11 split by sketch profile, another body's face or the body's own face | Sheet built from a planar region or extruded profile as the split tool; face-of-solid as tool. | [4.3](#p-4-3) |
| I01 projection into sketches, edge imprint; M12 wrap/emboss | Curve projection and imprint; arc-length mapping onto developable faces. | [4.5](#p-4-5), [7.4](#p-7-4), [B73](#b73) |
| I01 ellipse and spline sketch entities; S07 symmetry of entities | GCS ellipse/spline entities with point-on and tangent constraints; entity-level symmetry composed from the existing `Symmetric` point constraint. | [B75](#b75) |
| M08/M09/M13 section-cut interference, clash, curvature/zebra/draft overlays, measure depth | Clash with witnesses, silhouettes, draft maps, entity-pair minimum and maximum distances with witnesses, wall thickness. | [7.5](#p-7-5) |
| M10 non-uniform scale | Qualification and disclosure of the anisotropic transform path, which rewrites analytic carriers as NURBS; non-hemisphere sphere patches are sampled fits. | [B74](#b74) |
| D01–D04 drawings | Section faces and projected visible/hidden polylines only; sheet, view and annotation models are consumer scope. | `section` (face handles with boundary wires), `projectEdges` (decision row) |

Consumer-only scope from the same comparison, recorded so it is not re-asked:
datum planes and axes, keep-originals on booleans and transforms, hotkeys,
saved views, item groups, history step actions, expression units and typed
variables, reference images, DXF/SVG/PDF writers and readers, auto-boolean
inference, and every drawing-sheet construct.

### File-interoperability consumer overlay (2026-09-29)

OpenZCAD's [format specification](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/blob/1043003f69feeb44db862b84586d4a0ee43c7373/docs/plans/file-format-interoperability.md)
separates application readers/writers from kernel geometry work. The native
and IGES qualification contract is [specified here](native-format-interoperability.md).
Select bounded work under the owners below without displacing the correctness
priority lanes or reopening completed geometry rows.

| OpenZCAD scope | Needs from Remus | Owner here |
| --- | --- | --- |
| I02/I04 STEP fidelity and protocol choice | Assembly reader/writer and bound occurrence identity; names/colors; qualified AP242 writing. A UI protocol choice is not writer support. | [O5.1](#o-o5-1a), [O5.2](#o-o5-2), [O5.3a](#o-o5-3a), [O5.4](#o-o5-4) |
| I10 translator evaluation; I11 Parasolid, I12 ACIS, I13 Rhino | Design a bounded exact B-rep/metadata bridge, decide independent reader versus optional external translator, and qualify native/packaged WASM imports/exports by version and surface family. No current XT/SAT/3DM reader is claimed. | [O5.6](#o-o5-6); [O4.6](#o-o4-6) for serialized compatibility, [2.0c](#p-2-0c)/[2.6](#p-2-6) for authoritative boundary/scale contracts |
| I14 SolidWorks; I15 Inventor; I16 JT; I17 Creo; I18 NX; I19 CATIA; I20 Solid Edge | Reuse the translator boundary; native application parsing stays external unless independently justified. Preserve assembly identity and source-to-result geometry; JT mesh-only input stays mesh-only. | [O5.6](#o-o5-6), [O5.1](#o-o5-1a)/[O5.4](#o-o5-4), [O5.5](#o-o5-5) for responsibility boundaries |
| I06 IGES import/export | Existing IO APIs are present but IGES remains declared lossy preview. Inventory supported entity/unit/trim/shell behavior, qualify independent fixtures, then record any narrowly justified scope change before a precise-geometry claim. | [O5.7](#o-o5-7); existing IGES decision below remains the default |
| I08 PLY export | Existing writer/WASM API, with consumer export controls and independent-reader parity; demonstrated translator defects return as bounded kernel witnesses. | Consumer I08; [O1.5](#o-o1-5)/[O4.2a](#o-o4-2a) for source/package evidence |
| D04 DXF, I07 SVG, D01 PDF, I09 USDZ | Application parsing/writing, sketch/sheet models and visualization packaging; ellipse/spline constraints and sheet/wire representation keep their existing kernel owners and qualification limits. | Consumer scope; [B75](#b75), [4.2](#p-4-2)/[4.7](#p-4-7) when a consumer needs those representations |

External-part selection, upload consent, cloud retention, desktop execution,
licence purchase and service deployment are consumer decisions. Kernel planning
does not authorize proprietary SDK integration into the default Apache-2.0
build or imply source feature-history reconstruction.

## Dependencies and horizons

The horizon labels are capability gates, not dates or versions of the current
committed package. In particular H4's “v1.0” means the first stable public-publish
milestone; the existing 2.x package numbering is a different release channel.

| Horizon | Work and prerequisite | Exit / next gate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| H1 — Current | The priority lanes above, preserving landed M2/M4/M5/M6 subsets. | Qualify one bounded slice at a time; close only its declared row scope. |
| H2 — Generality | Finish 2.4 integration; then M3.4–3.6, O2.1c–e and O2.3b–d. O2.2 stays inside the M2 lane; O2.4 follows 2.6. | Curved intersection/arrangement and tolerant modeling satisfy their specification gates. M4.1–4.7 already have bounded implementations; 4.8 remains. |
| H3 — Modeling depth | M5 remainder, M6 generalization and M7. Keep 7.4 early for general re-limitation; the bounded 6.1 implementation already exists. | Imported-body edits, blends, offsets and surfacing have both positive oracles and typed boundary refusals. O1.3b follows M5; O5.3c follows PMI read; O7 is design-only after the body-model gate. |
| H4 — Stable public release | All release gates below; O4.2c remains an explicit publish decision. | Qualified release head, reproducible evidence, reviewed capability bounds. |
| H5 — Core modeling parity | H4 plus the full authored-modeling gates below. | Equivalent-quality results with the O1.2f baseline pinned. |
| H6 — Industrial interchange and corpus parity | H5 plus real supplier-data and resource gates below. | Published corpus, interchange, lifecycle and concurrency evidence. |
| H7 — Demonstrated leadership | At least five LC1–LC13 claims meeting the evidence discipline, including two correctness claims (LC1–LC4) and one browser claim (LC7–LC9). | Repeatable independent evidence, equivalent quality, published losses and ongoing regression gates. See the [claim definitions](industrial-parity.md#7-leadership-claims-and-the-discipline-they-must-meet). |

### Scheduling constraints

- Contract and installed-package qualification begin together. Boundary and
  independent-oracle gates precede promotion of broader blend/offset families;
  a named consumer-blocking regression may be fixed immediately with its own
  witness. Existing performance baselines and completed subsets remain valid
  within their recorded scope.
- O2.3b–d and 2.4/2.5 share the splitter. An integration slice waits for its
  prerequisite geometry contract; do not run overlapping changes independently.
- M3 tolerance integration and M2 share GFA acceptance bands. Stop and re-stage
  if integration destabilizes the qualified boolean matrix.
- O3.2 caching follows operation-local preparation and complete mutation/restore
  invalidation. O3.4 / PERF-D01 follows that substrate and shared-edge dependency
  planning (PERF-D03). B29 local transactions preserve all failure-atomicity and
  stale-handle contracts. Compaction (8.6 / PERF-T06) follows its versioned design.
- Native parallelism (8.3/8.4) follows boundary ownership and deterministic commit
  order. WASM threads (8.7 / PERF-W07) additionally need the consumer deployment
  decision. SIMD is a measured experiment, not a prerequisite for ordinary work.
- Original Open Kernel wave labels remain in the register as dependency groups:
  A = potentially independent foundation work, B = integration after its named
  prerequisites, C = later publication/PMI/hybrid scope. A is not proof of readiness;
  check the individual specification and live file ownership.
- O5.1 assembly exchange and O5.4 occurrence identity are coordinated stages.
  O5.2 attributes precedes the full O5.3 AP242/PMI claim. Publishing, hosting and
  outreach retain the explicit decisions listed below.

### H4 release gates

No completion counts are asserted by this consolidation. Each release candidate
must attach a fresh evidence checklist for these four gates, including the
[contract, installed-package and independent qualification evidence](#qualification-gates-for-the-priority-lanes)
for the domains claimed by that release:

| Gate | Required evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Modeling | EXIT-B1–EXIT-B5 below pass as permanent integration tests. |
| Adoption and proof | S1–S7 below have reproducible published evidence. |
| Capability | No unresolved cells remain in the capability matrix’s Known Unsupported-untyped / Partial inventory; attach evidence for every disposition. Do not silently weaken this release gate. |
| Remaining work | Every unfinished bridge row is closed or explicitly re-triaged for that release; a later horizon is not an implicit waiver. |

### P-Class exit benchmarks

The `EXIT-` prefix disambiguates these scenarios from bridge work; their scope
is unchanged from the former P-Class §5.

| | Scenario | Exit | Milestones |
|---|---|---|---|
| EXIT-B1 | Import two real freeform STEP bodies, fuse, fillet the intersection seam, export STEP | Re-import matches: watertight, valid, volume stable, names round-trip | M2 + M5 |
| EXIT-B2 | A gappy real-world import booleans correctly with zero heal invocations | Result tolerances disclosed and bounded by the context cap | M3 |
| EXIT-B3 | Shell a curved thin-wall part at a thickness that folds the inner offset | Self-intersection excised, valid result, volume vs. mesh oracle | M2 + 5.7 |
| EXIT-B4 | Direct-edit an imported body: move a boss across a filleted, holed plate | Neighbors re-limit; persistent references resolve Bound afterward | M6 |
| EXIT-B5 | The full boolean qualification suite at model scales 1e-5 through 1e6 | Exact or typed refusal at every scale — never a silent wrong volume | 2.6 |

### Open Kernel public claims

| # | Claim | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | Robustness leadership | ABC-scale scoreboard published per release; pass rate and trend public; zero silent-wrong classes open |
| S2 | Fillets that don't fail | Torture suite: 100% built-or-typed-refusal, 0 crashes; side-by-side with the incumbent's dispositions |
| S3 | Honest speed | Head-to-head harness public with wins *and* losses; never-silently-wrong as the headline |
| S4 | Three working doors | `cargo add` / `npm i` / `pip install` each to first solid in <10 lines |
| S5 | Interchange trust | CAx-IF round-trip with validation properties; AP242 assemblies + attributes |
| S6 | Someone else ships on it | ≥1 external consumer in production with their corpus in Remus CI |
| S7 | The naming demo | Direct-edit + persistent-ref survival, in the browser — the capability no other open kernel can show |

### H5 — Core modeling parity (authored-from-scratch domain)

Purpose: prove parity across the in-scope authored-modeling domain.

1. The [competitive crosswalk](industrial-parity.md#5-competitive-crosswalk) is complete: every in-scope row has a non-`Unknown`
   competitive state and an owner, and the crosswalk ownership rows are in this register.
2. Zero `Unsupported-untyped` cells in the capability matrix (carried from
   H4 and re-verified).
3. W1, W2, W5, and W7 complete correctly through both surfaces — not by
   refusal — at 1e-3/1/1e3 scale.
4. General curved booleans qualified: 2.4c/d, 2.5, 2.6, 2.7 closed; 4.8
   N-ary and mixed-dimensional cells qualified or typed with a named
   primitive.
5. Broad matrices qualified for fillet (M5 rows plus 5.8 rollover), chamfer
   (B3), shell/offset (5.7b, B5 done), draft (6.4), sweep (7.1), loft (7.2),
   direct edit (6.2, 6.3), with typed both-sides boundaries everywhere else.
6. Complete topology evolution for all covered operation families (B18 audit
   at zero unowned unresolved classes; 6.5 closed).
7. Stable Rust facade (O4.1c delegation, O4.2a/b dry-run green), stable
   JS/WASM contract (O4.4 registry and O4.7 typed results), and the planned Python surface (O4.3a/b)
   passing the mirrored contract suite.
8. Parity measurements published for the modeled-from-scratch scenarios
   (W1, W2, W5, W7) with O1.2f's baseline pinned and the [absolute gates](industrial-parity.md#34-absolute-gates-need-no-baseline)
   green; numeric bands: `[locked by O1.2f]`.

### H6 — Industrial interchange and corpus parity

Purpose: comparable outcomes on real supplier data, assemblies, and large
models.

1. W3 passes: dirty STEP models are diagnosed, tolerated (M3.5, EXIT-B2) or verified-repaired (B1), operated on (M3.4), and re-exported
   with complete disclosure; zero heal invocations on the tolerant path.
2. AP242 product structure (O5.1, O5.3a), attributes (O5.2), and the
   declared PMI read profile (O5.3b) qualified against CAx-IF test-round
   models (O1.4b).
3. Assembly occurrence identity and instancing stable (O5.4) across
   round trip and edits.
4. Real-model gauntlet stage pass rates within locked parity bands
   `[locked by O1.2f]`, with the taxonomy breakdown public (O1.1c/d).
5. Zero `silent_wrong`, `crash`, and unbounded `hang_or_budget_overrun`
   across the gated corpus on both kernels' comparison rows.
6. Imported-model boolean, blend, offset, direct-edit, tessellation, and
   measurement stages within bands `[locked by O1.2f]`.
7. Large-model memory and tail-latency budgets enforced (8.2, 8.6, O3.2,
   O3.4) with W8 passing under a declared memory ceiling.
8. Deterministic concurrent sessions and supported parallel operations
   (8.3, 8.4 under the 200-run determinism gate; 8.7 decision recorded).
9. A second serious external consumer exercising the native or Python API
   with its corpus in Remus CI (O6.3, S6).

scope; related performance packages are child slices, not independent capability
promotions. Original stabilization B1/B2/B3 use the `STAB-` prefix in the
consolidation map below.

All oracles independent (closed forms, implicit scans, both-geometry re-evaluation); near-tangent/tangent tolerance behavior recorded, never widened. **Bezier-clip seeds closed (2026-09-25, PR #642):** `clip_to_fat_line` re-used the parent segment's control polygon at every depth, so each clip was a fixed centred shrink (traced: [1/4, 3/4] of the window per level on the minimized seed, true root A-u 0.5378 left A's window at depth 7, sampled-AABB prune at depth 8). `math/nurbs/bezier_clip.rs` now blossoms both windows from their parent segments at every depth, clips an affine fat-line functional through the weighted (rational) numerators, and terminates/polishes/merges and splits overlap from tangent contact in model space (second-order tangent/curvature agreement); the tolerance-padded degenerate-AABB exit is kept (conservative control-point boxes). Evidence: both crossing seeds un-ignored with closed-form oracles at 1e-9·scale (circle twins; the ellipse seed was rebuilt with an explicit X major axis — its old ellipses were disjoint, so it never reached the clipper); the tangent cell now pins exactly one hit on the closed-form contact at every scale, tol 1e-7 and 1e-9, three contact angles, external and internal (was scale-1 only: phantoms at 1e-3, a 6.7e-4-off duplicate at 1e3, 4 fragments at tol 1e-9); math unit tests pin the minimized seed, window blossoming, input-order parameters across role swaps, and the `bezier_clip/cubic_pair` bench pair (3 sign-change-certified crossings; main returned 0 hits), all failing on main; that bench runs −54% (62→28 µs). No other workspace crate calls the clipper. **Conic-distance seeds closed (2026-09-25, PR #658):** `Parabola3D`/`Hyperbola3D` implement `ParametricCurve` (unbounded domain, documented), and the trait gains a defaulted exact `derivative_pair` (NURBS + all four conics). `geometry/extrema` `point_to_curve` and `curve_to_curve` dropped the residual-times-curvature term (Gauss-Newton): 2x overshoot at the ellipse minor vertex (orbit), 3x at a hyperbola vertex (divergence, 20% distance error at 1e-3), singular matrix for parallel closest tangents (line vs parabola vertex, 2x distance error at 1e-3); both now take a safeguarded full Newton step, backtrack on distance or a halved stationarity residual (distance alone resolves the foot only to sqrt(eps)), and converge relative to the range. Two seed premises were wrong and are corrected against closed forms: the parabola/hyperbola queries lay beyond the vertex's centre of curvature (the vertex is a local distance maximum there), and the ellipse twin is exact — the seed read ~3 of implicit residual in a fixed x-major frame while `Ellipse3D::new` puts the major axis on +Y. `operations::distance` routes ellipse/NURBS/conic edges through `point_to_curve`; its suites pass. Remainder: analytic CC/CS arms, quartic-seam cells, NURBS×NURBS matrix (shared with 2.4c/2.5), cone/sphere/torus sampled-twin approximate cells. |

Follow-up (2026-09-21, this slice): result-aware completeness in `evolution.rs` (`completeness_for_result`/`accounts_for_result`/`is_resolved_for_result`/`is_construction_resolved_for_result` plus `CompletenessReport` with omitted/phantom/unresolved) preserves `is_complete()`'s unresolved-only contract; `regress_evolution_result_aware.rs` proves the checker on synthetic omitted/phantom/unresolved/empty/split/merge/multi-parent/deleted maps and qualifies `split_with_evolution` per half at 1/1000/0.001x, under translation/rotation, with validation, closed-form volumes and failed-split rollback. Audited draft/shell-closed/offset/fillet/boolean-fuse/pattern through the new API with no failures; synthetic maps prove the checker, not a production defect. Follow-up (2026-09-25, this PR): **default V2 offset family closed F/E/V.** New `boundary_evolution` module induces every result edge/vertex claim from an exact face map by incidence (unique source → `Modified`, split pieces each attributed; none → `Generated` from the meeting faces; shared → `Unresolved` with typed `UnresolvedReason`, e.g. `ambiguous_incidence`) and `EntityCompletenessReport` checks faces, edges and vertices against the actual result sets; `offset_solid_v2_with_entity_evolution` / `offset_journaled_with_entities` refuse unaccounted history, `offset_journaled` now journals F/E/V (box: 26 events, was 6), and WASM `offsetJournaled` adds an additive `evolution` field (events, typed reasons, completeness) identical across direct, `executeBatch` and `executeBatchV2`. Offset geometry unchanged (bit-identical to the plain path). `qualify_offset_entity_evolution.rs` qualifies box at 1e-3/1/1e3 and cylinder/cone at 1e-3/1/10, outward/inward, origin and rigid placement, against a nearest-entity oracle (role oracle for seams, which the engine rebuilds 90° away), plus dropped/phantom records, split/merge attribution through journal resolution, typed-unresolved torus seams and sphere equator rings that fail closed, and atomic refusal. Found and filed B55 (curved offsets refuse ≥100 units, pre-existing). Follow-up (2026-09-26, this PR): **journaled linear-pattern family closed F/E/V.** `linear_pattern_with_entity_history` carries copy-time `(source, copy)` edge/vertex pairs from `copy_solid_with_entity_map` (never coordinates or allocation order); `linear_pattern_journaled` records original F/E/V Modified-into-themselves and copies Generated from same-kind source, zero Preserved/Deleted/Unresolved, Construction origin, each subject under its own EntityKey kind. `regress_pattern_evolution_fev.rs` qualifies count 1/2/3, disjoint/touching, box/cylinder/hollow-box at 1e-3/1/1e3 plus rigid placement, with result-entity census, original-only Bound naming, arena round-trip/restore/subsequent-edit, typed refusals with rollback, and direct/batch WASM parity (`linear_pattern_journaled_resolves_all_kinds_direct_and_batch`); legacy `(CompoundId, EvolutionMap)` and `{"compound","op"}` shapes and material results unchanged. Follow-up (2026-09-26, this PR): **journaled circular-pattern family closed F/E/V.** `circular_pattern_with_entity_history` carries copy-time `(source, copy)` edge/vertex pairs from `copy_solid_with_entity_map` (never coordinates or allocation order); `circular_pattern_journaled` records original F/E/V Modified-into-themselves and copies Generated from same-kind source, zero Preserved/Deleted/Unresolved, Construction origin, each subject under its own EntityKey kind. `regress_circular_pattern_evolution_fev.rs` qualifies count 2/3/6, disjoint/touching, box/cylinder/hollow-box at 1e-3/1/1e3 plus consistent rotation, with result-entity census, rotated positions/carriers, original-only Bound naming, arena round-trip/restore/subsequent-edit, typed refusals with rollback, and direct/batch WASM parity (`circular_pattern_journaled_resolves_all_kinds_direct_and_batch`); legacy `(CompoundId, EvolutionMap)` and `{"compound","op"}` shapes, minimum count 2, origin-based axis and material results unchanged, grid outside scope. Follow-up (2026-09-27, this PR): **journaled grid-pattern family closed F/E/V, native-only.** `grid_pattern_with_entity_history` carries copy-time `(source, copy)` edge/vertex pairs from `copy_solid_with_entity_map` (never coordinates or allocation order); `grid_pattern_journaled` records original F/E/V Modified-into-themselves and copies Generated from same-kind source, zero Preserved/Deleted/Unresolved, Construction origin, each subject under its own EntityKey kind. `regress_grid_pattern_evolution_fev.rs` qualifies 1x1/1xN/Nx1/multi-row/column grids, box/cylinder/hollow-box at 1e-3/1/1e3, touching/disjoint, consistent body+direction transform with translated row/column geometry, original-only Bound naming, arena round-trip/restore/subsequent-edit, and typed refusals (counts/spacings/directions/stale-handles/overlap) with rollback and stale-slot growth; legacy `(CompoundId, EvolutionMap)` placement/validation/overlap unchanged. Browser exposure (direct/batch WASM) is a separate remaining integration task; this slice does not claim the grid family or B18 complete. Follow-up (2026-09-30, this PR): **extrusion family closed F/E/V.** `extrude_impl` now returns its construction record (caps, side faces, longitudinal/copied/split edges, shared/copied/split-new vertices by source identity, never allocation order or coordinates); `extrude_with_entity_evolution` translates it into an exact face map (both caps `Modified` from the profile, every side wall `Generated` from it) plus boundary claims (shared entities `Modified` into themselves, split pieces and translated copies `Modified` from source, longitudinal edges and split-new vertices `Generated` from the profile), gated by `EntityCompletenessReport` against the actual result sets; `extrude_journaled` (kind `extrude`, plus `Model::extrude_journaled` and additive WASM `extrudeJournaled` direct/batch with the shared `evolution` envelope) records all three kinds. `regress_extrusion_evolution_fev.rs` qualifies polygons, holed profiles, circles, ellipses, supported conic/NURBS profiles, reversed traversal, positive/negative direction and closed-edge splitting (outer loop and hole) with result-entity censuses, an independent geometric oracle per claim, dropped/phantom checker proofs, split `BoundMany` naming, subsequent-edit/arena/checkpoint evidence and atomic refusals; legacy `extrude` signature, geometry (STEP-identical) and material results unchanged, and no profile class needed a typed-unresolved witness. Direct/batch/native parity pinned in `extrude_journaled_has_direct_batch_parity_census_and_rollback`. Remaining, audit order: shell boundary maps, split/section boundaries, then revolve/sweep/loft caps; faces-only fillet/chamfer and plane split; offset torus-seam/sphere-ring disambiguation (needs engine intersection-phase edge records), arc-joint and self-intersection-removal provenance. See [audit](evolution-audit.md). Arena-history correctness follow-up (this PR, in flight): appending an exact arena document preserves destination operation IDs and selections, rebases imported IDs/ordinals, and scopes imported global barriers to imported entities. Fresh-session IDs and bytes are preserved; source-document references require explicit rebasing when appended to an existing session. Regression coverage includes parent chasing, translator documents, real unjournaled gaps, retired namespaces, malformed rollback and native/WASM entry points; this does not close the remaining evolution families.  **Browser grid-history integration (2026-10-21):** additive `gridPatternJournaled` adds a bounded direct/`executeBatch`/`executeBatchV2` surface over the existing native producer. **Tier: core** because it edits live topology handles and the session evolution journal used by persistent references. Local native and optimized fresh/installed package qualification passes complete result F/E/V census and same-kind construction history, strict new-API counts, original-only references, pre-operation checkpoint restore, subsequent-edit references and typed atomic refusals (runtime source `afb738c2d`, +5,728 kernel bytes, translator unchanged); [audit](evolution-audit.md#browser-grid-pattern-history-2026-10-21). **Bounded consumer adoption:** [OpenZCAD #807](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/807), reviewed at `96079458035ce8562dfa778eaac1bdcd09b4da7f` and merged at `2cbf82edc0f75a97025d28e620cb7041cd99187f` after [head checks](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/actions/runs/39616051752) and [merge-group checks](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/actions/runs/39619832382) on that same merge candidate, pins both `2026.1.71` packages at `b464097112a6a25878666740442a46eb54b0ed7a` and adopts grid face and qualified-edge history. Producer-order claims are verified before members and claims are permuted together into the application's existing order. **Runtime native-vertex consumer follow-up (2026-10-21):** [OpenZCAD #812](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/812), merged at [`ba01d714`](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/commit/ba01d7143e454f5bf35a8d147afb4dce3571a147), and [#816](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/816), merged at [`dda20274`](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/commit/dda2027457e31b4bcac999e7047d876f0d26bd59), deliver qualified runtime native-vertex evidence for eligible single-source, non-fused GRID and LINEAR translations on the same paired `2026.1.71` pin. Current construction-origin metadata must match the actual unique F/E/V event count; edge/vertex subject sets and extra no-match sentinels must be complete, and all supported incident named-open-edge anchors must independently select the same actual native vertex under the authored transform. Public output ordinals do not expose `Generated.sources`. GRID keeps producer-order checks before application-order publication and its existing multi-source qualified-edge behavior; multi-source LINEAR adds neither edge nor vertex ordinal scans, and neither family adds multi-source native vertices or fused output relations. The shared cap of 4,000,000 event visits withholds an unaffordable vertex lane while retaining existing edge/face names, geometry and legacy endpoint datums. Runtime names and exact native points use transient body-scoped ids; persisted R01 sources remain named edges with canonical end 0/1, and advertised contradictory native evidence refuses. Closed/seam anchors, vertices that coincide within the witness quantum in one solid, and stale or conflicting anchors remain unqualified; touching members retain distinct vertices. Cold replay, count/spacing edits, checkpoint undo/redo and deleted-member refusals are covered. Exact merged-candidate CI retained [#812 E2E suite (725 total cases)](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/actions/runs/39674029883) (701 first-attempt passes, 24 permitted skips, no flakes) and [#816 E2E suite (728 total cases)](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/actions/runs/39687736473) (702 first-attempt passes, 24 permitted skips, two retry-passed flakes, no unexpected failures). Historical public readbacks verified health, build metadata and both pinned WASM assets; they do not establish production browser journeys. Persisted native vertex references, native vertex inheritance across multiple source solids, multi-source LINEAR edge inheritance, fused relations, history through collapsed multi-solid modifiers and other downstream evolution families remain open; B18 and consumer K05 remain **Partial**. |

(2) `compute_sphere_v_range` chose the pole by the rim's signed area projected onto the WORLD XY plane — for a frame rotated about X or Y that projection is a degenerate line whose area is rounding noise, opposite for the two hemispheres near the origin and identical far from it, so both faces swept the same half (`per-face tris [10011, 0]`); it now winds about the sphere's own axis. | <a id="b49"></a>B49 | **Mid-wall boundary runs bridged by chords through a cylinder or cone wall** — `interior_grid_resolution` hard-codes two interior rows for a developable band (issue #259 pins a filleted box at that density). When the boundary leaves the two rims — a notch rim, a boolean section curve, a stepped rim — the dense mid-wall run is bridged to that single row by triangles long in `u`, and those chords cut through the solid. The mesh stays closed and manifold, so no watertightness or non-manifold oracle sees it; only an area/volume comparison against the closed form does. Measured 2026-09-19 on the B37 pointed-cone–cylinder fuse (after B37's two fixes): the signed mesh volume does **not converge** — 7.063715 / 7.099480 / 7.137545 / 7.141582 at deflection 0.1 / 1e-2 / 1e-3 / 6e-5 (1168 → 64888 triangles) against an exact 7.286577, i.e. still 2.0 % low and flat at the finest deflection, while the cut leg of the same pair converges normally (4.145971 vs 4.144984 at 6e-5). (1) Coplanar-cap family: the cone's top rim is TANGENT to the box's `y = 1` top edge at `(0.5, 1, dz)`, so the true cut is two lumps touching at one point. Fix (algo builder): a plane face's OPEN curved sections split at its boundary pave junctions (`split_plane_curved_sections_at_boundary_junctions`), as curved faces already did at their wire vertices; closed circles keep their hole machinery, and a junction within the weld band (100·tol) of the section's own endpoint is that endpoint, not a split point (the tangent-boss rim ends at the exact tangency while EF paves the wall edge 1.6e-6 away — splitting there minted a micro-edge and broke `regress_boss_crossing_a_wall`'s exact tangent cell and the `tangent-boss-operand-retention` repro bundle during development). The honest result — 9 faces, two lumps sharing one vertex — then met three operations-gate gaps it had never exercised: whole-solid Euler counts the shared vertex once (3, not 2·2; the ops validator already counts per component), the cut-safety probe sampled a crescent lobe's box centre (inside the cone), and the disjointness narrow phase clashed the untrimmed parametric rectangle `tessellate_with_uvs` gives a trimmed cone wall. The multi-region gate now adds the shared-vertex surplus (`shared_vertex_surplus`), re-probes a centre the component does not enclose from a ray-parity-certified interior point (`component_interior_probe`), clashes trim-honouring component meshes (`tessellate_closed_face_set`) and exempts contact confined to a shared vertex (`triangle_surfaces_intersect_off_contacts`); nesting probes a non-shared vertex. Corner-touching cubes, which refused `ExactOnlyUnattainable` on main for the same Euler reason, now fuse exact. The split also exposed the r0 = 2 siblings (cone widening upward: one lump, tangency only at the rim): EF solved the box edge's grazing contact with the cone wall 1.6e-7 along the edge from EE's exact vertex (sqrt-of-residual), and the section and the rim split at different points; EF now snaps a tangential contact onto an extra pave already on the same edge under the existing incidence check (`edge_extra_pave_within`). (2) Fine-mesh family: the cut B-Rep was right; the cone wall's non-planar CDT drew a dense trim row inside the vertex-merge cell of a base-grid row at d ≈ 7.70e-5–7.82e-5, the two samples welded into one mesh vertex and the triangles between them repeated edges (87 non-manifold, zero duplicate triangles). Fix (tessellator): one interior sample per merge cell, boundary samples first. No tolerance, healing or mesh-fallback change. Evidence: `regress_b53_finding17_neighborhood.rs` (7 tests + 1 ignored ready-repro, 6 red on unmodified main; the general-position dy = 0.9/1.1 neighbours pass on both): every member exact, analytic (planes + cones), strict-valid on both validators, watertight at 0.1/0.01/1e-4/harness deflection, translation-invariant, the three legs' fine-mesh volumes satisfying inclusion–exclusion to 1e-5 of the stock and the cut matching an independent exact slice integral (disc ∩ rectangle per z) to 1e-5, ray-cast probes in both lobes and out of the removed cone; the pinned witness is two lumps sharing exactly one vertex with the top split one face per lobe. Both witnesses un-ignored (3/3 runs). Exhaustive sweep (`b53_finding17_neighborhood_sweep`, 17,150 inputs, full `check_bool_pair` battery): 23 fail → 0, no panic, exact legs fuse 6,417 → 6,420, intersect 6,882 → 6,882, cut 6,840 → 6,866 — so the generation exclusion is lifted (`is_finding17_neighborhood` now only enumerates the sweep). Remainder, pinned or disclosed, none a wrong answer: the r0 = 2 tangent cut's cone wall meshes sparse below d ≈ 8e-5 (the dense trim rows exceed the boundary-test budget and are skipped: 25,328 → 563 triangles, watertight but 0.6 % volume-heavy; harness deflection clean) — ignored ready-repro `b53_single_lump_cut_fine_mesh_volume`; box(3, 1, 2.5) × cone(3, 3) refuses typed on main and here (the wire builder discards the corner lobe's cone wall where the rim also crosses the x = 3, y = 0 corner edge 0.007 above the floor). Discovered, not chased (measurement layer, present on main): `mass_properties` on these NURBS-trimmed cone walls reads up to 1 % high on the smallest pieces and moves under rigid translation, and `solid_volume` takes that Gauss route on the general-position dy = 1.1 neighbour (0.866525 vs exact 0.866153, 0.27 % translation drift, inside the B26 1 % slack). |
| <a id="b54"></a>B54 | **Open-mesh route mixing in `solid_volume`, and planar CDT Steiner cracks between holed planes (OpenZCAD growing-holder witness, 2026-09-24)** — bumping OpenZCAD's kernel pin from `cb13b53f` to `bdb44304` failed `test/growing-holder-recipe.test.ts`: it grows a countersunk 60×32×20 U-bracket's opening 44 → 60 → 30 → 10 through box-mask intersects, a rebuilt floor extrusion and one `fuseAll`, and asserts `volume(solid, 0.001)` changes by 160 mm² × Δwidth. The x and y placements read −5411.3547 / −5411.3544 at width 10 for −5440 (the z placement passed). Two roots, bisected at the phase boundaries: (1) **tessellation** — the floor cap and the arm's inner face (holed by the boss footprint) are both planes with holes, so both are triangulated up front as planar CDT jobs; at width 10 the cap's flip recovery stalled on their shared 24 mm edge and Steiner-split it at the midpoint, and the splice that shares such a point only reached faces meshed AFTER the jobs, so the arm face still spanned the edge in one triangle — a T-junction, three open mesh edges at deflections ≤ 0.01 (present on `cb13b53f` too; the B-Rep is strict-valid and its Gauss volume identical on both pins); (2) **measurement** — the countersink cones are trimmed by the arms' side faces (hyperbolas stored as NURBS), so since [B32](#b32) the body measures on its closed whole-solid mesh, and the OPEN mesh silently fell through to the analytic bounding rectangle the body had been routed away from, 28.9 mm³ heavy, while every neighbouring width measured on the mesh (on the old pin every width took the rectangle, so the constant error cancelled out of the differences). The adapter measures `volume` at 0.08 in its boolean, fillet and feature checks, so the route mixing reached production comparisons. Fixes: `tessellate/solid.rs::split_triangles_spanning_boundary_splits` records every CDT job's boundary Steiner points per shared segment, merges both sides' points into one chain, and splits any job triangle spanning chain members (winding kept, no new vertex); `measure/volume.rs::required_closed_mesh_volume` is the one decision for all five closed-mesh gates (scalloped sphere collar, torus notch band, non-latitude sphere patch, NURBS / NURBS-trimmed-quadric direct path, torus bore beside a NURBS-rimmed plane): closed mesh → its volume; open → the order-8 boundary-trimmed Gauss integral over every face (the `mass_properties` route) when every face is on a qualified Gauss path, otherwise a typed `Unsupported` (scalloped collars, unqualified torus trims, period-winding notched walls). The final generic path still takes an unchecked mesh volume; no per-face route is known wrong there. | `crates/operations/tests/regress_volume_open_mesh_route.rs`, `wasm/src/bindings/growing_holder_volume_tests.rs`, `operations/src/tessellate/solid.rs`, `operations/src/measure/volume.rs` (unit tests `open_required_mesh_*`), `operations/src/tessellate/tests.rs` (`boundary_steiner_*`) | S | A silent-wrong volume on a strict-valid consumer body, and a crack class every pair of holed planes can hit. | <a id="b56"></a>B56 | **`solid_volume` signed tetrahedra cancel far from the origin (found closing B40, 2026-09-25)** — the whole-solid route (`signed_volume_from_mesh`, and the per-face `volume_from_per_face_tessellation` sum) takes each tetrahedron about the world origin. A 1e-3 cone–sphere result moved by the B26 harness offset (13, −7, 5) keeps a watertight mesh of identical shape and locally referenced volume, yet reads up to 0.7% off on every leg (e.g. witness cut 5.8646e-10 → 5.8236e-10): terms of order \|offset\|³ cancel to a 1e-9 result. The B26 translation oracle cannot see it (its 1e-6 absolute `VOL_FLOOR`). | `operations/tests/regress_b40_conesphere_small_scale.rs::b40_solid_volume_far_translation_small_scale`, `operations/tests/regress_b56_volume_local_reference.rs`, `operations/src/measure/volume.rs` | S | Translation invariance is the doubled-boundary detector; numeric drift in the detector hides or mimics real defects at small scale. Measure layer, overlaps open PR #622. Exit: the ignored repro greens (sum about a local reference point). | **Done (2026-09-25):** every triangle sum in `measure/volume.rs` now takes its tetrahedra about the centre of the summed points' bounding box when that is reference-free: the whole-solid mesh (`signed_volume_from_mesh`, `oriented_solid_volume`), the planar-triangle fast path (`solid_volume_from_faces`), and the triangle-face centroid (`center_of_mass_from_faces`) go local exactly when every directed edge is cancelled by its reverse (`directed_edges_cancel`, `summation_point`). A mesh with a hole, or with a face wound the wrong way, has no reference-free volume and keeps the historic origin sum, so no such reading changes. The suite showed why: three bodies read right only because their defect lies in a plane through the origin, where it contributes nothing. The slotted no-lip bin body (`io/tests/slotted_nolip_fuse_inmem.rs`) tessellates at 0.05 with 48 edges run twice the same way on its x = 0 / y = 0 planes, so its fuse read 135237.5 about the origin but 89185.6 about its box centre. The B17 Off-policy box keeps its injected flipped normal on z = 0. The `stl::import` 8-vertex box fixture passes one +z normal for every vertex, which flips its z = 0 face inward. None of these is fixed here; each still reads its old value. The slotted body has since been repaired as [B59](#b59), and [B60](#b60) tracks 11 more fixtures with the same signature. The per-face sum uses one local reference for all faces (its pieces never share vertex indices, and its chord-sized seam cracks then cost the same at any placement), and so does the legacy tessellated centroid. The direct route mixes mesh faces with analytic terms taken about the origin, so it keeps the origin but splits each determinant exactly as det(a−r, b−r, c−r) + r·((b−a)×(c−a)), which cuts its error from ε·\|r\|³ to ε·\|r\|·L², the analytic terms' own order. About the origin each triple product carried an ε·\|offset\|³ rounding error against an L³ answer. Evidence: witness un-ignored (3/3 green); `regress_b56_volume_local_reference.rs` (a 1280-triangle icosphere B-Rep at the harness offset against its polyhedral volume and symmetric centroid, plus sphere and frustum whole-solid meshes moved vs in place, at 1e-3/1/1e3) and `measure::volume::local_reference_tests` (per-face and whole-solid sums of a moved 1e-3 sphere, the split identity, and the edge-cancellation choice on a closed, a flipped-face and a holed cube). With the old sums the witness, both new integration tests and the per-face unit test fail: the icosphere volume read 4.6e-5 off, the sphere mesh 2.3e-3, the per-face sum 2.0e-4, and the triangle-face centroid landed 0.18 radii from the centre. Remainder, check layer, filed as [B58](#b58): the Gauss integrator behind `mass_properties` is also referenced to the origin. |

On a closed body the volume is the same about any point, so in-place readings move by round-off only: over the sweep below, mass, volume and area in place change by ≤ 6e-14 relative; the cross-drilled shaft's tensor moves 5e-11 (its old origin sat at the shaft's base). A body whose boundary does not close has no reference-free volume, and it now reads its own gap about its own centre instead of hiding it about the origin: the suite's one such body is filed as [B62](#b62). Evidence: `regress_b58_mass_properties_local_reference.rs` sweeps box, B-spline-converted box (NURBS quadrature), sphere, frustum, pointed cone, torus and the cross-drilled shaft (cylinder bands trimmed by ellipse arcs) at 1e-3/1/1e3, each moved by the harness offset scaled with the body (≈1.3e4 body lengths), against the same body in place, through `mass_properties` and `solid_center_of_mass`: worst mass 2.4e-11, centroid 3.5e-12 body lengths, tensor 2.7e-11 relative (the B-spline box). | <a id="b79"></a>B79 | **Closed section loop crossing a planar face's existing hole (gauntlet all-planar family, F2 selection 2026-10-02)** — a planar face that already carries an inner wire (a rib or boss footprint left by a fuse, or a pocket rim left by a cut) receives a tool footprint that closes strictly inside its OUTER wire but crosses the hole. `plane_internal_line_loops` admits the loop on the outer polygon alone, never testing the original inner wires, so `split_face_with_internal_loops` carves the square as an independent hole and keeps the old hole (split at the crossings) running through it: overlapping holes, free edges inside the tool square, the square's edges across the feature mouth used three times. Three cells in the recorded 26-cell approximate gauntlet census are this family. The broader scratch dispatch also promoted `mambo-simple-s31`; the qualified line-boundary repair leaves it approximate. Exit: the five original `b79_*` repros green with their assertions unchanged (`ExactOnly` quality, strict operations validity and the check-crate supplement except `ShellConnected` (legitimate disconnected lumps), all-plane census, 0 free / 0 over, Gauss volume equal to the inclusion–exclusion closed form within 1e-9, watertight manifold meshes at 0.1 and 0.01, ray-cast material probes; the bar, cross-rib and pocket through-cuts also run at (10, −10, 10) and at ×1e3, while the other cases run at the origin at unit scale), the controls kept green, b35/b36/b37 exact in the smoke gauntlet, and the full splitter foil set, workspace suites, `cargo test -p remus-wasm --lib gridfinity` and `approx_census` re-run. | **Done in source (2026-10-13, this PR, line boundaries):** the internal-loop gate now receives the original inner wires and tests deduplicated `Line` sections against hole wires whose curves are all `Line`. Fresh optimized paired WASM and installed tarballs pass 99 combinations of eleven fixtures, three API routes and three placements/scales with independent box volumes, strict validation, B-Rep/mesh closure, material probes and preserved operands. | <a id="b81"></a>B81 | **Planar faces with recognized NURBS arcs integrated the wrong arc (Fuzz Smoke 2026-10-04, `modifier_ops` crash-acf0ccca)** — a 1×3×1 box fused with a torus (R 6.5, r 2.5) turned π/6 about X and moved to (0, −1, −1.5), then filleted, read 1457.80 by `mass_properties` against 804.27 by `solid_volume`. Replayed natively, the fillet was innocent: the fused base body already read 1457.48. The torus bites into two box faces and every section arc on them is a NURBS that `recognize_curve` identifies (x = 0 holds the torus axis, so its arcs are two r = 2.5 meridian circles; the z = 1 spiric arcs fit ellipses within the 1e-6 check), which sends both faces down the exact Green's-theorem path. A bite runs clockwise in the face frame: the recognized-circle arm always took the counter-clockwise span and integrated each arc's ~2π complement (face area 41.76 for 2.49), and the recognized-ellipse arm measured its span as an angle about the face's first vertex with swapped `atan2` arguments and used it as an ellipse parameter (664.0 for 2.20). Fix (owning layer `check/src/properties/face_integrator.rs` only): one `accumulate_recognized_arc_green_segments` takes the span in the recognized curve's own parameter between the NURBS points at the trim ends, with the direction chosen so the traced NURBS midpoint (quarter point when closed) lies on it, and refuses the exact path instead of silently dropping an edge without a usable trim. | `check/tests/regress_planar_recognized_arc_direction.rs`, `operations/tests/regress_box_torus_fuse_mass_properties.rs`, `fuzz/corpus/modifier_ops/fillet-after-box-fused-with-tilted-torus-meridian-bite` | S | A silent 81 % mass error through the public measurement route on a two-primitive fuse. Exit: closed-form areas/volumes for clockwise bites, minor and major bulges, circle and ellipse, all four trim/use orientations at 1e-3/1/1e3 and an oblique off-origin placement (1e-9 relative); the fused body within 1e-6 of an independent oracle; seed replays clean. | **Done (2026-10-07, this PR):** both regressions fail before (area 1.38e-5 vs 3.59e-6 at 1e-3; mass 1457.48 vs 804.3472) and pass after. Independent oracle: Pappus torus + box − overlap, the overlap integrated by Gauss–Legendre over the box's x–z face with each column's y-interval from the exact line–torus quartic roots, 804.3471978352 converged to 13 digits at 100/200/400 points per axis; `mass_properties` now reads 804.3472063 (1e-8, the ellipse fit of the spiric arcs) and `solid_volume` 804.2661 (−1e-4, inscribed mesh). The reconstructed 26-byte seed decodes to the logged case under arbitrary 1.4.2 and, through a stable build of the `modifier_ops` target, reproduces the CI panic text before the fix and exits 0 after. Residual, not chased here: `fillet_v2` on this body takes ~21 s under the `ci-test` profile (the same run's `slow-unit-*` artifacts are the perf twin); the hyperbola and parabola arms take `project(start)`/`project(end)` and were not touched. |

| <a id="b85"></a>B85 | **Planar faces and extrude walls bounded by arcs past π were built inside-out (kernel review 2026-10-14, TOPO-1)** — `make_planar_face_from_wire` orients the plane by Newell's method over boundary samples. Those samples took an open conic arc the short way between its projected endpoints, so an arc past π traced its complement. They also ignored which way a single closed conic was used and reduced a NURBS edge to its chord. A counter-clockwise 270° arc closed by its chord (circle, ellipse or NURBS) got a −z plane, a reversed closed circle got +z, and `thicken(+1)` grew the body below the profile. Separately, `extrude` oriented each cylinder wall from the chord at the arc's start, which turns against the tangent past π, so even a correctly oriented 270° profile extruded inside-out (0.952 against 2.856). Reached through WASM `makeFaceFromWire`, `extrude`, `thicken` and `offsetFace`. | `topology/src/builder.rs`, `operations/src/extrude.rs`, `topology/tests/regress_wire_plane_arc_orientation.rs`, `operations/tests/regress_arc_profile_orientation.rs` | S | Silent material inversion on any profile with a major arc. Exit: the face normal follows the wire's winding and the extruded walls point outward for every arc length. | **Done in source (2026-10-14, this PR):** conic samples follow the authoritative traced span and traversal direction. NURBS winding is qualified across all trimmed knot spans using rational Bernstein curve-versus-chord area bounds; refinement stops once the total signed area exceeds its accumulated error, including exact analytic conic corrections for mixed boundaries. Degree above 32, more than 4,096 Bézier pieces per wire, and unresolved winding refuse before a planar face is allocated; nonplanar general-construction fallbacks remain available. Extrude reads qualified planar-wire winding and orients circular walls from the actual curve tangent at the witness point, so nonuniform rational parameters need no angular-midpoint assumption. Coverage includes the positive-area knot-localized profile that four uniform samples inverted, shared trims, degree elevation, natural non-clamped domains, reversed traversal and scale controls, mixed thin circular/polyline loops, ambiguous refusal without a face allocation, nonplanar fallback, native thicken/extrude, nonuniform trimmed circle walls with closed-form volume, and reachable `executeBatch` regressions. Three prior builder tests now carry explicit trims where they intend the short arc; raw untrimmed conics retain their counter-clockwise reconstruction convention. |

| <a id="b87"></a>B87 | **Hole-clearance guard compared the radius, not the setback (kernel review 2026-10-14, BLEND-3)** — `reject_blend_into_hole` refused only when a hole sat within the radius `r` of the edge, but a rolling ball touches each planar neighbour `r / tan(α/2)` from the edge (α the dihedral on the ball's side), i.e. `r·tan(θ/2)` with θ the angle between outward normals, for convex and concave edges alike. That exceeds `r` on every edge sharper than 90°. A 60° wedge with a blind r = 1 hole 2.5 from the edge, filleted at r = 2 (setback 3.46): `fillet_v2` and the cascade returned `Ok(Walking)` with the cap's outer wire at x = 3.46 crossing the hole. Fix (`operations/src/blend_ops.rs`): `fillet_contact_setback` gives the plane–plane setback (sharing `planar_setback_per_unit`, the `tan(θ/2)` that B82's `blend_reach` now also reads), `f64::INFINITY` when the faces fold onto each other (θ → π, setback unbounded, so any hole refuses) and → 0 as they flatten (θ → 0); the guard compares the clearance with `max(setback, r)` and reports `clearance / max(tan(θ/2), 1)` as the available radius. The floor at `r` stays because the rolling-ball rebuild trims `r` away on an edge it cannot classify as convex or concave, so an obtuse edge keeps its old bound; a curved neighbour keeps the `r` comparison too (its setback also depends on curvature; not chased). | `operations/tests/regress_blend_hole_setback.rs` (`sixty_degree_wedge_fillet_reaching_the_hole_is_refused`, `right_angle_fillet_with_the_same_clearance_still_succeeds`, `obtuse_edge_keeps_the_radius_bound`), `blend_ops::tests::fillet_contact_setback_follows_the_dihedral` | S | Same silent self-crossing cap on any acute edge. Exit: the 60° wedge refuses at r = 2 (available 2.5/√3) through `fillet_v2` and the cascade with the arena unchanged, and fillets at r = 1.3 (setback 2.25) without crossing; the 90° edge with the same clearance fillets at r = 2, removing (1 − π/4)·r²·L within 5 %; a 120° edge refuses r = 3 (exact setback 1.73, radius 3, hole at 2.5) and fillets at r = 2; the setback is exact at 60°/90°/120° and bounded/unbounded at the flat/folded limits. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the wedge regression fails on main (`Walking` result) and passes after. Residual, not chased here: `independent_blend_groups` still splits a selection at `2·size` rather than the dihedral-aware reach, so two acute edges between `2r` and `2r·tan(θ/2)` apart are treated as independent features. |
| <a id="b89"></a>B89 | **Surface interpolation missed its own data on non-uniform grids (kernel review 2026-10-14, WASM-7 and MATH-10)** — `interpolate_surface` fitted each row and each column at its own chord-length parameters and knots, then built the tensor surface with row 0's and column 0's knots, and took the column parameters from the row fits' control points rather than the data. Unless every row and column happened to share one spacing, the surface did not pass through its data: a 3×3 grid with rows at x = 0, 1, 3, columns at y = 0, 2, 2.5 and the centre raised to z = 1 missed that point by 0.0432 (closest-point distance). It also passed unclamped degrees to `NurbsSurface::new`, so degree 3 on a 3-row grid failed with `InvalidDegree` where curve `interpolate` clamps. Reached through offset (`offset/src/offset.rs` NURBS refit), sweep side faces, `offset_face`, `offset_trim`, `untrim`, the anisotropic sphere-patch transform and WASM `interpolateSurface`. Fix (owning layer `math/src/nurbs/surface_fitting.rs` only): global tensor-product interpolation (NURBS Book A9.4) with one averaged chord-length parameter vector and one averaging knot vector per direction, shared by every row and column fit; zero-length lines (poles) leave the average; each degree clamps to n − 1. | `math/src/nurbs/surface_fitting.rs` (`interpolate_non_uniform_{3x3,4x5,7x4}_reproduces_data`, `interpolate_surface_clamps_degree_per_direction`, `interpolate_surface_with_pole_row_reproduces_data`, `interpolate_surface_regular_rows_keep_curve_knots`) | S | Every caller refits sampled or offset geometry through this one routine; a fit that misses its samples silently moves offset, swept and transformed faces off their intended geometry. Exit: every data point reproduced within 1e-9 on non-uniform 3×3, 4×5 and 7×4 grids at several degree pairs; degrees above n − 1 clamp. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** five of the six new tests fail on `main` at `0186a848` (3×3 miss 0.284 at the averaged parameters, 4×5 0.0212, 7×4 0.0308, pole grid 0.0332, clamp `InvalidDegree`) and all pass after. The sixth pins that a grid whose rows share one spacing keeps that row's own knots bit for bit (running mean). Downstream: the full workspace nextest (`ci-test`, 8,089 tests) passes unchanged, so no offset, sweep, transform or WASM assertion moved; the LSPIA bit-identity oracle covers curve LSPIA only and is unaffected. |
| <a id="b90"></a>B90 | **NURBS face area ignored the face's trim (kernel review 2026-10-14, OPS-4, confirmed on `main` 0186a84)** — `measure::face_area` (and so `solid_surface_area`, `sheet_surface_area`, `body_surface_area`, the WASM area bindings and the STEP reader's recomputed validation surface area) measured a NURBS face by tessellating its whole carrier patch through the standalone per-face mesher, which reads no wire. `convert_to_bspline` gives each planar face a carrier that overhangs it, so a converted 10³ box read 144 per face and 864 in total, and any NURBS face with an inner wire refused outright (the standalone mesher does not support holes). Fix (owning layer `operations` only): a NURBS face is measured by the triangle areas of its trimmed tessellation, the shared-edge pipeline run over that face alone (`tessellate_face_for_measurement`, a new open, normal-free measurement mode: no weld or gap fill), so the outer wire minus the inner wires is what is measured; the memo key now records the full boundary that mesher reads (wires, oriented edges with first-occurrence ordinals, curves, trims, vertex positions, per-use pcurves). Analytic and planar paths are untouched. Trimmed Gauss quadrature (`remus_check` `integrate_face`) was measured and rejected as the route: exact on planar patches, but on seamed NURBS carriers it reads a converted cylinder wall 0.7 % low and a converted cone wall at 0.0099 for 56.2. | `operations/src/measure/area.rs`, `operations/src/measure/nurbs_area_memo.rs`, `operations/src/tessellate/solid.rs`, regression `crates/operations/tests/regress_b90_nurbs_face_area_trim.rs`, memo tests `operations/src/measure/nurbs_area_memo/tests.rs` | S | A silent 44 % per-face area error on every converted planar face, and a hard refusal on every holed NURBS face, through the public measurement route.
