Memo tests: a re-trimmed face and a new hole on a warm carrier miss, a moved boundary vertex misses, restoring content hits with the original bits. Corpus check against converged trimmed quadrature: `shapr_untrimmed_nurbs_domain.step` 9.7191 (old, +32 %) → 7.36283 (1.7e-6 at 1e-4); `shapr3d_hammer_holder.step` 42 NURBS faces 278.5184 at 1e-4 (old 278.5187, quadrature 278.5130); at OpenZCAD's 0.08 the 34 `fillet_like` areas in `tests/golden/data/o06/hammer_holder_features.golden` move (mean relative error to quadrature 1.83e-3 → 6.34e-4, closer on 28/34) and those lines were updated, every other golden line unchanged. Cost: the reading now costs what whole-solid measurement tessellation costs, ~20× the old carrier mesher on curved converted walls at 1e-4 (converted r2 h7 cylinder wall 0.25 s → 5.7 s release), comparable on the hammer holder (6.2 s → 6.8 s at 1e-4) and faster at 0.01. Residuals, not chased here (each is a separate defect, and the whole-solid mesh shares it): `openzcad_e_nurbs_fillet_plate.step` bounds its parabolic bands with straight chords up to 1.06 mm off the carrier, so the trimmed mesh bridges the chord gap and reads 218.1 at 1e-4 against 194.79 for the bands alone (STEP-recomputed surface area moves with it); a converted sphere's two hemisphere faces each mesh as the whole sphere carrier (49.14 each; the solid mesh gives 49.14 and 0); a converted torus meshes on a fixed grid at 191.59 for 197.39 at every deflection; `remus_check` trimmed quadrature misreads seamed NURBS walls (above), which also skews `mass_properties` on converted cylinders and cones; the standalone `tessellate::tessellate` still skins a NURBS face's whole carrier for its other callers.Validation: `remus-operations`, `remus-wasm`, `remus` and `remus-io` nextest under `ci-test` 4,530 tests, all green after the golden update (the only failure before it was that golden); workspace all-target Clippy `-D warnings`, `wasm32-unknown-unknown` build, boundary/det-hash/doc-path scripts, and the 52-row semantic `approx_census` unchanged. |
| <a id="b250"></a>B250 | **The all-planar-triangle volume and centroid routes ignored holes (kernel review 2026-10-14, OPS-6)** — `solid_volume_from_faces` (the `solid_volume` fast path) and the face-based centroid read every planar face whose outer wire is a line triangle as that triangle alone and never looked at `inner_wires()`. A tetrahedron pierced by a triangular tunnel keeps triangle outer wires on both faces the tunnel opens, so it took the route, credited both openings (279.0 against 261.0 at unit scale) and, with the openings' edges missing from the directed-edge census, summed its tetrahedra about the world origin, so the reading also moved with the body (the review measured 154.17 against 165.74 for its translated tetrahedron; the centroid read 24.9 units off at (37, −91, 13)). Fix (owning layer `operations/src/measure/volume.rs` only): one `SignedTriangle::of_solid` gathers both routes' triangles and adds a fan over each line-bounded hole, wound against its face's triangle from geometry rather than the stored hole direction; the fan diagonals cancel, so the hole edges cancel against the tunnel walls and the local B56 reference applies again. A hole with a curved edge declines the route as before. | `operations/tests/regress_b126_pierced_triangle_volume.rs` | S | A silent volume and mass-centre error on mesh-like bodies with planar holes, through the public measurement route. Exit: closed-form volume and centroid (tetrahedron minus a vertical prism under the slanted face, its moments by the edge-midpoint rule, exact for the quadratic integrands) at 1e-3/1/1e3, in place and at (37, −91, 13), with either stored hole winding (1e-9 relative); `classify_point` puts the tunnel axis outside and the material beside it inside. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the volume and centroid regressions fail on `main` (279.0e-9 vs 261.0e-9 at 1e-3; centroid 24.9 off) and pass after; the material probe passes on both. |
| <a id="b251"></a>B251 | **`thicken` grew faces along the wrong normal (kernel review 2026-10-14, BLEND-8)** — `thicken` extruded every face along its carrier's normal at parameter `(0, 0)` and ignored `is_reversed`. A curved face was swept along that single direction instead of offset (the `y < 0` half-cylinder wall slid along +x and read 339.3 against 14π), a bored hole's wall (reversed) and a planar face carried against its plane normal grew into the material they bound, and a sphere or frustum face came back non-manifold or mis-sized. Fix (owning layer `operations/src/thicken.rs`): a planar face, or a NURBS face whose control net is planar, extrudes along its effective outward normal; a cylinder, cone, sphere or torus face becomes the shell between the face and its exact, frame-preserving offset carrier, closed by walls swept along the boundary normals — a plane, cylinder or cone per edge (the offset of a ruling, a parallel, a meridian, or any circle on a sphere, each certified on samples), seams skipped, closed coplanar rims as annuli, and a face of seams alone (`make_torus`) thickened to a hollow tube whose original face is the cavity shell; `analytic_torus_signed_volume` now reads such a whole-torus face over its full window instead of returning 0 for its single-vertex one. Loops are built in effective order and stored through the kernel's `is_forward() != reversed` reading. Everything else (elliptic or NURBS rims, curved NURBS faces, an offset collapsing the carrier, a cone face at its apex, a torus tube reaching its axis) is a typed `Unsupported`, and a non-finite thickness is `InvalidInput`. | `operations/tests/regress_b128_thicken_normal_field.rs` | M | A public modeling operation returned silently wrong solids for every curved or reversed face. Exit: closed-form volumes (annular sectors and tubes, hemispherical shells, a frustum wall by Pappus) through `mass_properties` (1e-6) and `solid_volume` (1e-3), `classify_point` probes on both sides of the source face, `validate_solid` clean, both thickness signs, reversed faces, a bored-hole wall from a real `Cut`, a whole `make_torus` face, and an oblique off-origin placement. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** all eight regressions fail on `main` (wrong side, non-manifold walls, volumes up to 7.7× off) and pass after. Residual, not chased: elliptic/NURBS rims (a cylinder cut obliquely) and curved NURBS faces refuse rather than offset; their walls are exact ruled NURBS on a cylinder (affine radial scaling) and a sphere (homothety), the natural next cell. |
| <a id="b252"></a>B252 | **Transforms left face pcurves in the old UV chart (kernel review 2026-10-14, OPS-2)** — `transform_solid` and `copy_and_transform_solid` moved vertices, edges and carriers but never touched pcurves. The plane chart has no stored frame (origin at the foot of the world origin, x axis `normal × seed`), so it is rebuilt from the moved plane; cylinder and cone frames are rebuilt canonically from the axis, dropping the imported reference direction. On the imported analytic fillet plate a translation by (10, 0, 0) made `write_step` refuse a plane pcurve that missed its vertex by 10, a rotation by 1.46, and mirror and uniform scale failed the same way. Fix: each surface arm reports a `ChartMap` and `reexpress_face_pcurves` carries every boundary use into the new chart. Planes take an exact 2D affine map between the two canonical charts (lines rescale their parameter, conics become a native circle or principal-axis ellipse with `t ↦ ±t + φ`, NURBS map their control points). Every carrier is still built exactly as before (booleans are tuned on where the canonical cylinder and cone frames put the seam; keeping the moved reference direction instead turned a B52 box–cylinder fuse into `ExactOnlyUnattainable` and gave B68 two extra faces). Revolved carriers instead map `(u, v) ↦ (±u + φ, s·v)`: `φ` is the angle of the moved zero direction in the rebuilt frame, the sign is negative under a reflection (the rebuilt frame is right-handed), and `s` scales an axial or slant `v`. Sphere and torus frames are carried, so a rigid motion leaves their pcurves bit-exact and a mirror negates `u`, which was previously left stale. Periodic lift counts are re-derived from the moved geometry by the rule the STEP reader stamps and the writer certifies, since a seam vertex can project to 0 or 2π − ε after any motion. Charts replaced by NURBS still drop their pcurves, now also clearing the lift count (a winding without its pcurve failed boundary authority). Found alongside: the STEP reader lifted each pcurve endpoint independently next to the 2D curve's reference, and an endpoint exactly half a turn away tied onto the far branch. Two of the plate's band pcurves swept three quarters of the cylinder on import (sampled `SameParameter` 6.0, the band diameter; the review's "cylinder off by 6.0 under translation" predates the transform). A declared pcurve range is now accepted when its ends hit the oriented vertices modulo the period (it is the pcurve's own trace). A derived LINE range (an unbounded LINE pcurve, the practical derived case) is settled geometrically: each end's lift must lie on the line, which leaves one lift for an oblique line; a line along a periodic axis is a monotone sub-turn isoparametric arc, so the end is the first on-line lift in the direction the use leaves its start (the edge's analytic tangent against the surface partials). Other derived pcurves unwrap along the edge's trace in use order: a uniform grid plus every interior NURBS knot, bisected wherever a step jumps more than a quarter period, within a depth and sample budget (a bounded refinement, not a certificate). | `operations/src/transform.rs`, `operations/src/transform/pcurves.rs`, `operations/src/copy.rs`, `io/src/step/reader.rs` (`lift_pcurve_endpoint_branches`), `io/tests/regress_transform_pcurve_charts.rs` | S | A pure translation of an imported body could not be exported, and every downstream consumer of pcurves (STEP, the arena gate, `SameParameter` proofs) saw geometry up to the full move away from its edge. Exit: on the fixture, translation, rotation, mirror, uniform scale and anisotropic scale, in place and by copy, keep every surviving pcurve tracing exactly `M ×` its original trace, band `SameParameter` ≤ 1e-7, boundary authority valid, and a `write_step` → `read_step` round trip with volume preserved. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the fixture regression fails on main (import `SameParameter` 6.0; transform trace mismatch and `write_step` refusal) and passes after. Unit oracles in `transform/pcurves/tests.rs`: `new_surface(map(uv)) = M · old_surface(uv)` to 1e-13 relative on a 9×9 chart grid for plane, cylinder, cone, sphere and torus under translation, oblique rotation, mirror, uniform scale and scaled mirror; every carrier stays bit-identical to the pre-change construction, and sphere/torus pcurves stay bit-exact under proper maps; line, circle, ellipse and rational NURBS images match at every normalized parameter under rotation, reflection, scaled rotation, shear and anisotropic reflection (circles stay circles exactly when conformal); lift-count rule and replaced-chart cleanup. Not changed: reflected NURBS faces still drop their pcurves (their u-reversal is affine and could be re-expressed instead), and the plate's rational-B-spline plane pcurves stay as imported, on their arcs but not angle-linear (0.16 at mid-span against the circle's parameter, before and after any transform). |
| <a id="b253"></a>B253 | **The cascade fallback skipped the B71 material guard, and the rolling-ball adjacent-extent guard measured the radius (kernel review 2026-10-14, BLEND-2 and BLEND-3 remainder)** — [B86](#b86) and [B87](#b87) put the hole guard in `fillet_cascade`'s rolling-ball fallback and made it measure the contact setback. Two parts of the findings remained. BLEND-2: the fallback still ran `planar_fillet_result` bare of the lazy B71 material guard (`attempt_with_lazy_material_guard`) that `fillet_v2` gives its own rolling-ball attempt. BLEND-3: Phase 2b of `fillet_rolling_ball_with_origins` refused only when the radius reached the length of the adjacent edge on a support face, but the band's contact line runs one setback, `r·tan(θ/2)` between planes, from the edge and must land on that adjacent edge. A 60° wedge whose face is 3 deep passed r = 2 (setback 3.46) and failed later as `trimming-failure`. Fix (owning layer `operations/src/blend_ops.rs`, `operations/src/fillet/rolling_ball.rs`): the fallback runs under the same material guard; Phase 2b compares `fillet_contact_setback` with the adjacent edge's perpendicular extent from the filleted edge (on a stripe the rebuild could not classify, never below r: its fallback lays contacts one radius out), and reports the radius whose setback meets it. Curved target edges and collinear continuations keep the legacy radius-against-length bound. | `operations/tests/regress_b124_rolling_ball_adjacent_extent.rs` | S | A band that runs off its support face reached a late postcondition failure instead of a typed cliff, and the fallback could commit a wrong-side band `fillet_v2` would refuse. The material-guard route has no standalone repro: no selection was found that `fillet_v2` refuses on material grounds while the bare rebuild accepts. |

| <a id="b255"></a>B255 | **`section` sectioned the untrimmed curved carriers and left cone loops open (kernel review 2026-10-14, OPS-5)** — the sphere, cone and torus arms of `section` took the plane section of the complete carrier (`analytic_intersection::intersect_plane_*`) and ignored the face's trims, so every curved patch contributed its whole carrier's section: a unit-sphere cap above `z = 0.5` yielded the full unit circle at `z = 0`, a phantom disc of area π where the plane misses the body; its `x = 0` section read 3.137 against the 0.614 segment; the B70 torus notch kept the notched-away tube loop (56.5 against 9π). A whole boundary wire lying in the plane keeps the loop (a sphere's equator, a flush rim); a section leaving its face away from every crossing, or a cone face reaching its apex or spanning both nappes, is `Unsupported`. | `operations/tests/regress_b125_section_curved_trims.rs` | M | A public query returned phantom or doubled sections for every trimmed sphere, cone or torus face, and failed outright on cones. Residual, not chased: the NURBS arm still sections the untrimmed surface, and the planar arm still ignores hole wires; the polylines keep their 64-chord sampling. |

| <a id="b259"></a>B259 | **Absolute volume floors on the closed-mesh routes (kernel review 2026-10-14, OPS-9)** — `solid_volume`'s closed-mesh gates (`closed_mesh_or_exact_volume`, the clamp retry in `required_closed_mesh_volume_with`, and the final whole-solid mesh) accepted a mesh volume only above an absolute `1e-12`, so the closed mesh of any body under about 1e-4 across was turned away and the body fell through to the routes those gates exist to avoid (the analytic rectangle, the per-face sum, a refusal). Residuals, not chased (outside operations/blend/offset): `remus-math`'s `Circle3D` and analytic-surface constructors keep `radius <= 0.0`; `mirror`, `split` and `section` do not check their plane point for finiteness. |
| <a id="b200"></a>B200 | **Persistent references bound retired entities and ignored an unjournaled tail (kernel review TOPO-2, 2026-10-14)** — `naming::resolve` chased the journal and mapped the surviving ordinals to arena keys without asking whether the keys were live or whether the model had changed since the newest entry. Deleting a face's solid without journaling it resolved `Bound { face }` on the retired face; a checkpoint restore that kept a post-checkpoint retirement did the same with no tail at all, because the journal and the tick count roll back while the retirement stays. Fix: a journal-anchored chase that reaches the present fails closed as the new `Resolution::Stale { reason }` (`ref_stale`, `invalid_input`; WASM `status: "stale"`) when the tick count disagrees with the newest entry (the global barrier the next `journal_begin` records, of unknown scope, so it severs untouched entities too) or when any bound key is not live; one retired piece of a split fails the whole set rather than binding the survivors. Signature anchors resolve against the live model and are unchanged. Breaking for exhaustive matches: `Resolution` and `TopologyError` each gain a variant. | `topology/src/naming.rs` (`an_unjournaled_tail_fails_closed`, `an_unjournaled_tail_severs_untouched_entities_too`, `a_lineage_ending_on_a_retired_entity_fails_closed`, `a_split_with_a_retired_piece_fails_closed`), `topology/src/lib.rs`, `wasm/src/bindings/naming.rs` | S | Wrong is worse than none: an attribute or a downstream feature must never ride on a face that no longer exists. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the first three regressions fail on `main` at `0186a84`; the eight synthetic-key resolver tests now resolve against a topology where their keys are live. |
| <a id="b201"></a>B201 | **Handle-preserving restore inside a live transaction truncated its rollback (kernel review TOPO-3, 2026-10-14)** — `restore_preserving_handle_slots` took the same-lineage prefix shortcut meant for exact restores: it truncated the live scope's undo records to the clone point, although it keeps post-clone retirements and re-promotes the faces they orphan onto fresh loops. A face whose loops went [0] → [1] (boundary rewrite in the scope) → [2] (restore) came back from the outer rollback on loop [0] without reviving it. On a clone that inherited its source's lineage, the same restore rolled the tick back to the clone point, so a later clone of it passed for an unchanged prefix of the source's log and a scoped `restore_for_rollback` of that clone dropped the source's records. Reachable in production: `operations::replace_surface` runs offset's `replace_surface_with_entity_map`, which restores a checkpoint on failure, inside `run_transacted`. Fix: the shortcut requires an exact restore (no slot live in the snapshot is retired here); an inexact restore records the full pre-restore state while scopes are live and rebases the lineage otherwise. | `topology/src/topology.rs`, `topology/src/undo_log.rs`, `topology/src/arena.rs`, `topology/src/transaction/savepoint_tests.rs` (`handle_preserving_restore_inside_a_scope_rolls_back_exactly`, `handle_preserving_restore_rebases_an_inheriting_clone`) | S | A rollback must return the exact entry state; a face on a dead loop breaks every later boundary walk. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** both regressions fail on `main` at `0186a84`. Only inexact in-scope restores pay the full inverse; exact ones keep the O(1) truncation. Review follow-up (pre-existing, both restore kinds): a snapshot cloned before a live scope opened passed the prefix check when log lengths coincided, so the rollback could not revive what was allocated in between; the shortcut now also requires every live scope to predate the clone (`restoring_a_snapshot_older_than_the_scope_rolls_back_exactly`). |
| <a id="b202"></a>B202 | **Refused `delete_solid` opened a spurious journal gap (kernel review TOPO-6, 2026-10-14)** — `delete_solid` counted a mutation tick before validating, so refusing a stale handle or a solid still held by a compound or comp-solid changed nothing yet made the next `journal_begin` record an `unjournaled_mutations` global barrier, severing every persistent reference (journal 1 → 2 entries before the next real one). Fix: the tick and cache bump move after reference discovery and the compound/comp-solid checks, before the first retirement. | `topology/src/topology.rs` (`refused_delete_solid_leaves_no_journal_gap`) | S | A refusal must be a no-op for history as well as for the arenas. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the regression fails on `main` at `0186a84`. |
| <a id="b203"></a>B203 | **Whole-document work in checkpoint restore and `delete_solid` (kernel review TOPO-4/5, 2026-10-14)** — `restore_preserving_handle_slots` built a carried-authority map (pcurve clones included) for every snapshot face, though only faces whose loops stayed retired read it, and cloned then filtered the pcurve index it rebuilds from scratch two steps later. `delete_solid` inserted every entity of every other live body into SipHash sets to find what the deleted solid shares. Fix: authority is read from the snapshot only for re-promoted faces and the dead index copy is gone; the retained side of `delete_solid` uses one flag per arena slot (dense, never-reused indices) with the same validating walk. Callgrind, `profiling` profile, 24/200 drilled-block documents (box minus cylinder): restore after a committed delete 3.32M → 1.49M / 25.06M → 10.97M instructions (−55%/−56%), restore of an unchanged-but-grown arena 2.73M → 1.66M / 21.17M → 11.26M (−39%/−47%), `delete_solid` 2.72M → 1.19M / 23.38M → 9.44M (−56%/−60%). These are the whole native calls behind WASM `restoreCheckpoint`, failed-operation rollback and `deleteSolid`. | `topology/src/topology.rs` | S | Checkpoint restore and rollback scale with document size on every undo; both now pay half. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** behaviour pinned by the existing restore, authority-carrying and `delete_solid` sharing/refusal tests. |
| <a id="b204"></a>B204 | **`fromBREP` accepted ids that do not name exactly one entity (kernel review 2026-10-14, WASM-4)** — the JSON import kept vertex and edge ids in maps keyed by `as u32`. A second vertex or edge declaring an id overwrote the first, re-pointing every reference at the later entity; an id of 2^32 + n aliased onto id n wherever it was declared or referenced; and inner-wire entries with unknown or unreadable edge ids were skipped, so the hole was dropped and the import still succeeded. A unit cube with one corner re-declared under an existing id imported with volume 2.0 and 0 validation errors; a duplicated edge left an orphan edge in the session. (A reported plane-normal default sub-claim did not reproduce and is not changed.) | `wasm/src/kernel.rs` (`from_brep_impl_unchecked`, `brep_id`, `brep_ref`; tests `json_brep_id_tests`), `wasm/src/error.rs` (`WasmError::InvalidBrepId`, `BrepIdProblem`) | S | Untrusted-document import through the public kernel API returned a wrong solid as success. Exit: duplicate, unknown and out-of-range vertex/edge ids refused with a typed error naming the document location, unreadable inner-wire entries refused, the session unchanged after every refusal, and well-formed documents (both inner-wire spellings) still import. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** ids parse as integers in `0..=u32::MAX` and resolve through one lookup that refuses unknown ids; a second declaration is refused as `Duplicate`; every inner-wire entry must resolve completely. `WasmError::InvalidBrepId { entity, id, location, problem }` maps to `invalid_argument` with `argument` and `problem` details. The duplicate, out-of-range and inner-wire regressions import successfully on `main` and are refused after; the unit-cube control keeps volume 1.0. **Import-budget follow-up (this PR):** `fromBREP` refuses input above 256 MiB or 3,000,000 JSON values before the transaction snapshot; nested arrays, unknown fields and duplicate keys consume the same budget. Indexed-mesh bindings check source bytes and generated arena bounds before cloning, and the optional core binding constructs refused meshes outside the session so repeated refusals retain no arena slots. Scratch arena serialization enforces the same byte budget before output grows; a valid tetrahedron with a tiny encoding budget refuses before any session mutation. Validated indexed meshes append only reachable welded vertices without replacing the live topology lineage, retaining checkpoint copy-on-write, prior journal gaps and reserved operation IDs; closed/open controls compare exact arena bytes and allocated slot counts, including unused welding representatives. Tiny-limit, overflow, malformed-number and repeated-refusal regressions accompany valid round-trip and tetrahedron-volume controls. Tier: core (`fromBREP` restores session topology), lazy:io (indexed-mesh translator). These deterministic input bounds do not establish a browser peak-memory guarantee. |
| <a id="b205"></a>B205 | **`executeBatch` read malformed optional arguments as their defaults (kernel review 2026-10-14, WASM-5)** — 54 optional numeric arguments across 17 arms (`makeSphere`, `makeTorus`, `extrude`, `revolve`, `mirror`, `convertToElementary`, `healSolid`, `repairSolid`, `classifyPoint`, `circularPattern`, `gridPattern`, `offsetFace`, `section`, `split`, `sewFaces`, `linearPattern`, `draft`) were read as `get_f64(..).unwrap_or(d)` or `get_u32(..).unwrap_or(d)`, which cannot tell an absent argument from a present one of the wrong type or range. `mirror` with `nx: "0"` mirrored about the YZ plane and reported success. `multiSectionSweep.ruled`, `projectEdges.hiddenLines` and the `unifyFaces` option of `fuseWithOptions` / `cutWithOptions` / `intersectWithOptions` did the same through `as_bool().unwrap_or(true)`, and `multiSectionSweep.params` dropped unreadable entries. | `wasm/src/bindings/batch.rs` (`get_optional_u32`; the arms above), `wasm/src/bindings/batch_optional_args_tests.rs` | S | A typo in a JS caller's argument became a silently different model. Exit: absent and `null` keep their documented defaults; every present but malformed or out-of-range value is an `invalid_argument` naming the key. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the arms read optional arguments through `get_optional_f64` / `get_optional_u32` / `get_optional_bool` (the `extrudeDetailed` / `revolveDetailed` twins already did), and `params` through `get_f64_array`. Contract tests drive all 59 keys with a string and an array through `executeBatchV2` and expect `invalid_argument` naming the key (each accepted on `main`), refuse out-of-range integers, and keep the default mirror plane for absent and `null` normals. |
| <a id="b206"></a>B206 | **Arena documents embedded the whole session journal (review IO-3, 2026-10-14)** — `serialize_journal` wrote `topo.journal().snapshot()` into every arena document and `deserialize_document` appends a document's journal on import, so a body serialized and deserialized back into the same session doubled the journal each round: one journaled fuse round-tripped six times grew from 1 to 64 entries and its document from 13,142 to 218,576 bytes. Every `kernel.serializeSolids` → `remus-wasm-io` export also carried unrelated bodies' history. Fix (option chosen over deserialize-side deduplication, which needs a session identity a cloned or restored topology cannot provide without risking wrong bindings, and over opt-in embedding, which leaves the duplication behind the flag): the writer embeds `Journal::lineage_snapshot`, the connected components of the document's entities in the entry–ordinal graph (ancestors and the descendants of shared ancestors, so a barrier on a split's other half still severs) plus global barriers after the earliest kept entry. Kept anchors resolve exactly as against the full journal; anchors outside the closure resolve `UnknownOperation`; a body no entry mentions carries no journal. High-water counters stay the source's, so a fresh load never reissues a source id. Schema, version, reader and append semantics unchanged. | `topology/src/journal.rs` (`lineage_snapshot_*`), `io/src/arena_io.rs::serialize_journal`, `io/tests/arena_journal_closure.rs`, `io/tests/{arena_append_history,naming_roundtrip}.rs` | S | Same-session copies and every translator export paid O(session history) in document size and, on re-import, exponential journal growth. Exit: same-session round trips append exactly the body's own lineage per import and re-export a document identical except for the two counters; history-free bodies round-trip byte-identically with the journal untouched; fresh-session resolution of every OperationOutput reference over the document's history matches a full-journal load. | **In review (2026-10-14, this PR):** the four `arena_journal_closure` regressions fail on `main` at `0186a84` and pass after; local nextest (`ci-test`): `remus-io` + `remus-topology` 1,233 passed, `remus-wasm` + `remus-wasm-io` 732 passed, the arena-using `remus-operations` targets 259 passed. Residual: a same-session append still advances the op/ordinal high-water counters by the source's counters (the append contract reserves the source namespace so rebased references never collide), so they double per same-session round trip of a body that carries history; overflow is a typed, atomic refusal after roughly 50 such imports. |
| <a id="b207"></a>B207 | **Inside-out meshes were never flipped on import (kernel review 2026-10-14, IO-6)** — `stl::import::import_mesh` flipped a closed mesh by its signed volume only when the mesh carried no normals, and otherwise trusted each triangle's stored normal. Every file reader supplies normals: OBJ, PLY, 3MF and glTF synthesise them from the winding, so they always agree with an inward winding, and a zero STL facet normal disagrees with nothing. An inward-wound OBJ cube, or an STL cube with zero normals, imported with signed volume −1 and `solid_is_inverted` true. | `io/src/stl/import.rs`, `io/tests/regress_mesh_import_orientation.rs` | S | Every mesh importer (`importStl`/`importObj`/`importPly`/`import3mf`/`importGlb`) could hand back an inverted body, which measures, classifies and exports inside out. Exit: a closed mesh imports outward whatever its stored normals. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** stored normals still correct individual triangles, then the orientation of the body comes from geometry: when every welded edge is used exactly twice in opposite directions, the mesh is flipped when every connected component encloses negative signed volume, each summed about one of its own vertices. Mixed component signs (a correctly wound cavity, or a partly inverted multi-body file one flip cannot fix), open and incoherent meshes keep the previous rule (no global flip with stored normals; origin-relative signed volume without). The inward OBJ, zero-normal STL and inward-normal STL cubes, at the origin and offset by (1000, −2000, 500), import with signed volume +1 and outward faces; they fail before the fix. Outward cubes, a mixed outward/inward pair and a cube with a cavity keep their winding, two inward bodies both flip, and an unused vertex at 1e20 cannot hide an inward cube. Tier: lazy:io (ships in the `remus-wasm-io` translators; the shipped kernel is built without `io`). |
| <a id="b208"></a>B208 | **3MF objects built from components imported empty (kernel review 2026-10-14, IO-4)** — the reader built one mesh per `<object>` from its `<mesh>` alone. An object made only of `<components>` came back as an empty mesh, component transforms were ignored, and the objects the components referenced came back as extra top-level meshes. `import3mf` in both binding modules then failed the whole file with "mesh has no triangles", which is how assembly-style files (a group of placed parts) arrive. | `io/src/threemf/reader.rs`, `io/tests/regress_threemf_components.rs`, `wasm-io/src/lib.rs::component_only_3mf_object_imports` | S | Valid 3MF assemblies could not be imported at all. Exit: a component-only object imports as one correctly placed mesh, and hostile component graphs are refused within `ImportLimits`. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** components resolve recursively with the 3MF row-vector transforms (translation in model units, scaled with the vertices), composed child-first; a mirroring transform keeps the winding outward. Objects that a component references are parts, so only unreferenced objects are top-level meshes, in document order; files without components are unchanged. Unknown, ambiguous and cross-part (production extension `p:path`) references, cycles and nesting deeper than 32 levels are typed `ParseError`s, and the flattened vertex and triangle totals are bounded by `max_model_entities` before any geometry is copied. Regressions fail before the fix. Build-item placement and root selection are completed by B209. Contacting components that form non-manifold triangle incidence are refused; general mesh union remains unsupported. Tier: lazy:io (ships in the `remus-wasm-io` translators; the shipped kernel is built without `io`). |
| <a id="b209"></a>B209 | **3MF `<build>` ignored: item transforms unapplied, roots independent of the build list (kernel review 2026-10-15, IO-4 follow-up)** — the reader never read `<build>`. `<item transform>` was dropped, so files that place objects on the bed through build items (PrusaSlicer and other slicers) imported every object at its resource origin; an object listed twice came back once, and resources no item names (and, before B208, component parts) came back as extra meshes. The 3MF Core spec says consumers output exactly the objects the build items name. | `io/src/threemf/reader.rs`, `io/tests/regress_threemf_build_items.rs` | S | Slicer-placed 3MF files imported at the wrong position with the wrong object set. Exit: one mesh per build item, in order and with multiplicity, placed by its item transform after component flattening, with the total output bounded by `ImportLimits`. | **Done (2026-10-15, this PR):** the roots are the build items, in build order and with multiplicity; each root is flattened as in B208 and then placed by its item transform (row-vector layout, translation scaled with the model unit, winding reversed under det < 0). A missing or empty `<build>` falls back to the B208 rule (unreferenced objects, document order). Build items count toward `max_model_entities`, and the flattened vertex and triangle totals are summed with item multiplicity and checked before any geometry is copied. Items naming an unknown or ambiguous object id, or using the production extension's `p:path`, are typed `ParseError`s. Namespaces are resolved: `build`, `item` and `component` count only in the core namespace (or unbound), only unqualified `objectid` and `transform` are read, and `path` is refused only when it resolves to the production namespace or an undeclared prefix, so extension markup such as `<v:build>` or `v:transform` cannot add or move meshes. Behaviour change for files whose build list differs from "every unreferenced object once": mesh count, order and position follow the build (both `import3mf` bindings return one solid per item). Regressions fail before the fix. Tier: lazy:io (a fix to the existing 3MF translator in `remus-wasm-io`; no new capability). |
| <a id="b210"></a>B210 | **The PLY reader ignored the declared header (kernel review 2026-10-14, IO-5)** — `ply/reader.rs` assumed `float x y z` vertices, took normals from columns 3–5 whenever any property name contained "nx", and read every face list as `uchar int`. Declared types, their order and any extra property or element were ignored. A binary PLY with `x y z` plus `red green blue` bytes read garbage vertices and dropped every face, and an ASCII PLY declaring `x y z red green blue nx ny nz` read its normals from the colour columns; both returned `Ok`. | `io/src/ply/reader.rs`, `io/tests/regress_ply_declared_properties.rs` | S | Common scanner and slicer PLY files (colours, quality, texture coordinates) imported as wrong geometry with no error, and the wrong normals then reoriented triangles in `import_mesh`. Exit: the body is read exactly as the header declares, or refused. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the header parses into typed elements and properties (all eight PLY scalar types and their sized aliases, any order, list properties with their declared count and item types), and the body is read by that declaration for ASCII and binary little-endian; unknown properties and elements are skipped by their declared sizes. Faces take `vertex_indices` or `vertex_index`. Every element count is bounded by `max_model_entities` at header time and every list length as it is read. Malformed declarations, values that disagree with the header (missing, extra, out of range, negative or out-of-bounds indices) and `binary_big_endian` are typed `ParseError`s instead of silent misreads. Regressions fail before the fix. Tier: lazy:io (ships in the `remus-wasm-io` translators; the shipped kernel is built without `io`). |
| <a id="b211"></a>B211 | **Legacy sketch `angle` coerced its second line (kernel review 2026-10-14, WASM-1)** — `parse_sketch_constraint` read `p3`/`p4` through `as_u64` with `p1`/`p2` defaults, so `p3: -1` or `p3: "2"` silently fell back to the first line's point and the constraint was applied to the wrong line, and `p3` without `p4` became `Angle(0, 1, 2, 1)`. | `wasm/src/helpers.rs` (`parse_sketch_constraint`, `json_usize`, `json_f64`), `wasm/src/bindings/sketch_validation_tests.rs` | S | A caller's typo constrained a different line than the one named. Exit: a present `p3`/`p4` parses strictly as an index, a lone `p3` or `p4` is refused, and the documented three-field form (`p3`/`p4` both absent or `null` → `Angle(p1, p2, p1, p2)`) is kept. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** both indices are read strictly when either is present; the parse returns `WasmError` so the refusal is natively testable. The refusal cases are accepted on `main` and refused after; the two accepted forms keep their stored constraint. |
| <a id="b212"></a>B212 | **GCS solve converged on a negative or collapsed ellipse semiaxis (kernel review 2026-10-14, WASM-2)** — ellipse semiaxes are free solver parameters and the implicit-form residuals read them only through `a²`/`b²`. An ellipse at the origin with `b = 1`, `phi = 0` and a fixed point at `(1, 0)` on it solved from `a = 5` to `a = −1` and from `a = 3` to `a = 0` (where the residual reads zero by the degenerate-axis contract), both reported `converged`; `write_params` published them, breaking the `EllipseData` invariant (finite, strictly positive). | `sketch/src/gcs/system.rs` (`publish_positive_ellipse_axes`), `sketch/src/lib.rs` (`SketchError::DegenerateEllipse`), `sketch/src/gcs/system/tests.rs`, `wasm/src/bindings/sketch_validation_tests.rs` | S | Downstream geometry built from a sketch read a negative or zero semiaxis as valid. Exit: no solve publishes a non-positive semiaxis; the mirror solution is published as its magnitude with a truthful residual; a collapsed semiaxis is a typed refusal with the pre-solve geometry restored. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** after every `solve` / `solve_detailed` of a sketch with ellipses, a negative semiaxis is replaced by its magnitude — the same point set; no residual can grow (implicit forms are even in each semiaxis, and for a positive target t, \|\|a\| − t\| ≤ \|a − t\| and \|\|a₁\| − \|a₂\|\| ≤ \|a₁ − a₂\|), and `max_residual`/`converged` are re-read when a sign changes. A semiaxis below the usable magnitude restores the pre-solve parameters and returns `SketchError::DegenerateEllipse`. Sketches without ellipses take no extra work. Native and `executeBatch` regressions fail on `main` and pass after. |
| <a id="b213"></a>B213 | **Legacy sketch stored unbuildable deferred constraints (kernel review 2026-10-14, WASM-8)** — `sketchAddConstraint` kept arc- and circle-referencing constraints as raw JSON and checked nothing, so a constraint naming a missing arc, circle or point, or missing a field, was accepted, and every later `sketchSolve` and `sketchDof` of that sketch failed; the legacy API has no way to remove it. | `wasm/src/bindings/sketch.rs` (`DeferredConstraint`, `sketch_add_constraint_impl`), `wasm/src/state.rs`, `wasm/src/bindings/sketch_validation_tests.rs` | S | One bad call permanently broke a sketch, reported far from its cause. Exit: such constraints are refused when added with the sketch unchanged, and well-formed deferred constraints still solve. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** deferred constraints are parsed into a typed `DeferredConstraint` and index-checked against the sketch's points, arcs and circles when added (legacy entities are append-only, so the check stays valid); solve time only maps indices to solver handles. Refusals are typed (`InvalidHandle` for a missing entity, `InvalidInput` for a malformed field). The regression's constraints are stored on `main` and refused after. |
| <a id="b214"></a>B214 | **Non-finite lengths crossed the STEP and arena boundaries (kernel review 2026-10-14, IO-8)** — the STEP reader checked each real for finiteness as it parsed it, then multiplied lengths by the file's unit scale, so a `.METRE.` sphere of radius `1E306` (1E309 mm) imported Ok with radius ∞, and a `1E306` m coordinate with an infinite vertex. Separately, the arena writer formatted a non-finite float the way `serde_json` does, as `null`, which the arena reader refuses: serialization succeeded and produced a document nothing could read back. | `io/src/step/reader.rs` (`UnitScale::length_mm`, `UnitScale::point_mm`), `io/src/json.rs` (`Writer::non_finite`), `io/src/arena_io/codec.rs`, `io/tests/step_arena_non_finite.rs` | S | Untrusted STEP could install infinite geometry through `importStep`, and a session holding non-finite geometry could export a document its own import refuses. Exit: no STEP length leaves the reader non-finite, and the arena writer never emits `null` for a number. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** every length the reader scales (points, radii, semi-axes, focal distance, vector magnitude, the validation-property centroid) is checked finite after scaling, and every built surface is checked finite as a whole, which catches derived overflow such as a plane offset `n·origin` or a swept plane; both are refused with `ParseError`; the JSON writer stays byte-identical to `serde_json` but records a non-finite write, and the arena encoder refuses such a document with `InvalidTopology`. All three regressions fail on `main` (sphere and point import Ok; serialize returns Ok) and pass after; the remus-io and remus-wasm-io suites pass. |
| <a id="b215"></a>B215 | **Sweep bindings and `Model::sweep` / `Model::pipe` leaked topology on refusal (kernel review 2026-10-14, WASM-6)** — the direct `multiSectionSweep`, `helicalSweep` and `guidedSweep` bindings and the facade's `Model::sweep` / `Model::pipe` ran their operation on the live topology outside a transaction (the `executeBatch` arms were already rolled back per op). A multi-section sweep with two sections at the same spine parameter fails in the loft after both profile copies are placed, leaving 8 vertices and 2 faces in the session; a pipe whose profile edge runs along the path fails on a side-face normal after the section rings are built (20 vertices, 36 edges, a wire and a face). | `wasm/src/bindings/operations.rs` (`multi_section_sweep_impl`, `helical_sweep_impl`, `guided_sweep_impl`), `remus/src/model.rs`, `wasm/src/bindings/sweep_transaction_tests.rs` | S | A refused call left orphan entities in a session that outlive the call. Exit: every one of the five entry points runs inside `run_transacted`; refusals leave entity counts unchanged and successful calls are unaffected. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the three bindings delegate to natively testable `_impl` bodies that wrap the operation in `run_transacted` (O(1) capture), and `Model::sweep` / `Model::pipe` do the same. The multi-section and pipe regressions fail with the wraps removed and pass after; the batch arm already matched. No input was found that makes `sweep` itself (behind `helicalSweep`, `guidedSweep`, `Model::sweep`) refuse after allocating, so those three wraps are covered by inspection only. |
| <a id="b216"></a>B216 | **Arena decode accepted analytic carriers no constructor could build (kernel review 2026-10-14, IO-7)** — the hand-written arena codec fills a carrier's private fields directly and only NURBS carriers were validated afterwards. A plane normal of `[0,0,0]` or `|n| = 2`, a cylinder or sphere of radius −1, a cone half-angle outside (0, π/2), a non-orthonormal frame, or a conic with a negated semi-axis (paired with a negated axis, so the endpoint certificate still passed) all deserialized Ok. | `io/src/arena_io.rs` (`curve_carrier_defect`, `surface_carrier_defect`), `io/src/step/reader.rs` (`PLANE`), `io/tests/arena_analytic_carrier_validation.rs` | S | Untrusted arena documents (`deserializeSolids`, every translator hand-off) could install geometry that downstream code assumes cannot exist. Exit: every analytic carrier is held to its constructor invariants on decode, and the writer refuses what the reader would. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** decode refuses with `ParseError` and encode with `InvalidTopology`: finite placement, unit and mutually perpendicular axes (1e-9, dimensionless), positive finite radii, semi-axes and focal length, cone half-angle in (0, π/2). Ellipse semi-axis order and torus R ≥ r are not required, matching `with_axes`/`with_axis`. Because WASM STEP import crosses the arena, the STEP `PLANE` normal is now rescaled to unit length (same plane; ISO direction ratios need not be unit); a declared zero normal still imports natively but no longer serializes. All 23 mutations decode Ok on `main` and are refused after; the remus-io suite passes. |
| <a id="b217"></a>B217 | **OBJ face lines were parsed whole before their triangle budget was checked (kernel review 2026-10-14, IO-9)** — `read_obj_with_limits` collected every corner of an `f` line into a vector before fan-triangulating it, so a single hostile face line was held in full before the budget refused it: an 8 MB line raised peak memory by 48 MB. | `io/src/obj/reader.rs`, `io/tests/obj_face_line_budget.rs` | S | Untrusted-import memory amplification through `importObj` under a lowered entity budget. Exit: nothing of a face line is parsed past the triangle budget. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** corners are fan-triangulated as they are split off, and each triangle is budgeted before it is stored, so no per-face vector exists. Peak memory is not observable in a test; parse order is: a four-million-corner face that only turns malformed after the budget is refused with `LimitExceeded { "OBJ triangles", 10, 11 }` after the fix and with the malformed corner's `ParseError` on `main`. Short faces, malformed corners within budget and a budget-sized fan keep their old results; the remus-io and remus-wasm-io suites pass. |
| <a id="b218"></a>B218 | **Mesh weld cells saturated far from the origin (kernel review 2026-10-14, IO-2)** — `stl::import` keyed its weld hash by `(coordinate / tolerance).floor() as i64`, which saturates once the ratio passes 2^63 (x = 1e12 at the 1e-7 default), so every vertex of a far-placed part shared one bucket and each insertion distance-checked every earlier vertex: a strip at x = 1e12 took 11.7× the time for 4× the vertices (3.1× near the origin). Cells are now keyed by the bits of the floored `f64` index, which never saturates, with the cell edge the smallest power of two ≥ 2·tol so the index is the coordinate scaled exactly: a pair within tolerance is at most one cell apart with no rounding. Once an index reaches 2^53 the coordinate's float spacing exceeds 2·tol, so only an identical coordinate can weld and that axis probes its own cell only. Keys depend on the point alone, so an outlier vertex cannot coarsen the cells of the rest (a bounding-box-sized grid would). Which positions weld is unchanged: every candidate is still distance-checked and the smallest vertex id wins. Witness: `stl::import` tests `far_from_origin_weld_stays_linear` (4,000,000 distance checks for the 4,000-position strip with the old key, ≤ 4 per position now, at x = 0, 1e12, −3e13 and −3e15), `far_outlier_vertex_does_not_coarsen_the_other_cells`, `overflowing_cell_indices_weld_only_identical_coordinates`, `power_of_two_cells_bracket_twice_the_tolerance`, `weld_over_the_full_f64_range_keys_finite_cells`, `pairs_just_inside_tolerance_weld_across_cell_boundaries`. | `io/src/stl/import.rs` | S | Every mesh format ends in `import_mesh`; a georeferenced or far-placed part made it quadratic. | In flight (this PR). |
| <a id="b219"></a>B219 | **STEP B-spline attributes were split without regard to strings (kernel review 2026-10-14, IO-10)** — `parse_bspline_curve_attrs` and `parse_bspline_surface_attrs` split their attributes on raw parentheses and commas, so the `(` in a name such as `'Curve (1)'` opened a phantom aggregate, shifted the control points onto `(1)` and refused a valid curve. Multiplicities were read by dropping any member that did not parse, and expansion zipped them with the knot values, so a multiplicity list longer or shorter than its knot list was truncated silently instead of refused. One string-aware scanner (`bspline_attr_layout`, `''` escapes included) now serves curves, 2D curves and surfaces; every multiplicity must be an integer, and a count that does not pair one-to-one with the knot values is refused by name (`check_knot_pairing`). Witness: `step::reader` tests `bspline_names_with_parentheses_commas_and_quotes_parse` and `bspline_knot_lists_must_pair_one_to_one`. | `io/src/step/reader.rs` | S | Names with parentheses are routine in exported STEP (`'Curve (1)'`, feature-tree labels); a dropped knot tail changes the curve. | In flight (this PR). |
| <a id="b220"></a>B220 | **A failing read-only batch op deep-copied the topology (kernel review 2026-10-14, WASM-3, perf)** — `executeBatch` runs `ReadOnly`-hinted ops against an O(1) `Rc` share of the topology, but on any error it restored through `topo_mut()` while that share was still alive, so `Rc::make_mut` deep-copied every arena (one `checkpoint.first-mutation-clone` span per failure) and the restore bumped the cache generation, invalidating persistent caches although nothing had changed. Fix (owning layer `wasm/src/bindings/batch.rs`): the read-only branch moves into `run_read_only`, which restores only when the topology pointer no longer equals the snapshot (every mutation under a live share goes through `make_mut`'s copy, so pointer equality proves no mutation). Tier: core (existing batch dispatcher; no new capability). | `wasm/src/bindings/batch.rs` (`failing_read_only_op_neither_copies_nor_invalidates_the_topology`, `read_only_hint_still_restores_an_op_that_mutated`) | S | Every refused query or `gcs*` call paid a whole-document copy and dropped downstream caches. Exit: a failing read-only op leaves the topology pointer and `cache_identity` unchanged with no clone span; one that did mutate still rolls back. | **Done (2026-10-15, this PR):** the first test fails on `main` (three clone spans for three failing ops) and passes after; the second guards the restore branch. |
| <a id="b221"></a>B221 | **IO fuzzing missed the sheet, validation, mesh-import and persistent-reference paths (kernel review 2026-10-14, IO-12)** — `step_reader` drove only the solid-only STEP entry point, the mesh targets stopped at parsing, and nothing decoded persistent references, although each path takes untrusted input. New targets: `step_bodies` (sheet-body and validation-property imports; refusals must leave no live entity, accepted imports return resolvable solids, sheet-class sheets and one report per solid), `mesh_import` (`import_mesh` over lattice meshes with degenerate, over-shared and mixed-winding triangles, far offsets and invalid indices; faces must be planar triangles, no edge used more than twice, input-validation refusals allocate nothing) and `persistent_ref` (decode, byte-stable re-encode to the same value, resolve without panic). Seeds: a validation-property box and a planar sheet written by the STEP writers, and operation-output, lineage and signature references from `serialize_persistent_ref`. CI's `Fuzz Targets Compile` job (main pushes, merge groups and `ci:full` PRs) compiles them; they are not scheduled yet, because `fuzz.yml` pins `fleet-fuzz.yml` to a `main` commit, so the matrix edit and the pin bump land as two follow-up changes. | `fuzz/` | S | The same review found defects behind these entry points (IO-6 in `import_mesh`, IO-3 in the history persistent references resolve through); fuzzing them catches the next one. | In flight (this PR). |
| <a id="b150"></a>B150 | **Degree-1 knot removal skipped its tolerance check (kernel review 2026-10-14, MATH-3)** — `curve_knot_remove` accepts a removal when its left and right reconstruction sweeps agree where they overlap. At degree 1 they do not overlap, so the check loop ran zero times and every interior knot came off: a polyline corner (0,0,0)–(1,1,0)–(2,0,0) with its knot at 0.5 was flattened to its chord at tolerance 1e-6, a deviation of 1.0. Reached through WASM `curveKnotRemove`. The certified `reduction` path calls it with tolerance `f64::MAX` and certifies the candidate itself, so it is unaffected. | `math/src/nurbs/knot_ops.rs`, `math/tests/regress_degree_one_knot_removal.rs` | S | A tolerance the caller passes must bound the result. Exit: every degree-1 removal is held to the Piegl & Tiller A5.8 bound; corners, off-parameter collinear points and rational corners refuse, chord-exact points and inserted knots still remove. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** at degree 1 the dropped control point is compared with its neighbours' homogeneous chord at `u` (A5.8's odd-count branch). With the excess E = (e, ε) the deviation is N(t)·|e − ε·C_new(t)| / W_old(t) ≤ max(|e − ε·P_{k−1}|, |e − ε·P_{k+1}|) / w_k over the whole span, which is held to the tolerance; for ε = 0 (non-rational, or an exactly inserted knot) it is the distance at `u`. A collinear point with a mismatched weight (x = 0, 1, 2, weights 1, 100, 1; true peak deviation 9/11) is refused below the bound 0.99, and a sweep of rational configurations checks every accepted removal against a sampled deviation. Closed-form oracles: corner deviation exactly 1.0 (refused up to and at 1.0, accepted just above it with the chord (1,0,0)); a collinear point off its parameter deviates 0.5; an interior corner of a four-point polyline deviates 3 and 1.5; a weighted corner stays 1.0; inserted knots (including the rational blend (1.5,0,0), weight 2) round-trip. The bound is evaluated in floating point without losing the deviation: relative to the previous control point (a 1e12 offset does not cancel a sub-ulp deviation), in weight ratios w/w_k (uniformly scaled weights from 1e-320 to 1e300 give the unit-weight answer, a tiny weight times a tiny offset does not underflow, and a ratio outside the normal range refuses), with a scaled norm (a 1e-200 deviation is refused at tolerance 0), at the stored knot rather than the caller's `u`, and a non-finite bound refuses. The bound also carries a forward error allowance for its own evaluation, 32u times the magnitude of its terms (about 1e-14 relative to the span), because the terms of e and ε can cancel: a removal is accepted only when the true deviation is certainly within the tolerance, so tolerance 0 refuses even an exactly removable knot. Fails on `main` for the original repro and every follow-up that asserts a refusal. |
| <a id="b151"></a>B151 | **Bezier decomposition and degree elevation assumed clamped end knots (kernel review 2026-10-14, MATH-4)** — `curve_to_bezier_segments` and `surface_to_bezier_patches` raise every interior knot to multiplicity `p` and then read each window of `p + 1` control points as a Bezier segment. That holds only where the domain ends also carry multiplicity `p`: on an unclamped knot vector the end control points are de Boor points, so every segment touching an end was a different curve, and `curve_degree_elevate` (built on the decomposition) returned it. A single-span unclamped uniform cubic elevated by one deviated by 2.47 (the review measured 2.03 on its own repro). Reached through WASM `curveDegreeElevate`, heal `convert_to_bezier`, `project_curve`, and the Bezier-clip curve–curve, curve–surface and SSI seeding paths that decompose their operands. | `math/src/nurbs/decompose.rs`, `math/tests/regress_unclamped_bezier_decomposition.rs` | S | Unclamped B-splines are legal `NurbsCurve`/`NurbsSurface` input (uniform and imported periodic-style knot vectors); silent geometry replacement in a representation change is a correctness defect. Exit: decomposition and elevation reproduce the input on its domain for unclamped curves and surfaces, per direction. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** each operand is clamped to its own domain first — both ends inserted to multiplicity `p` (Piegl & Tiller §5.2), then the knots and control points outside the domain dropped — per direction for surfaces. A curve or direction that is already clamped takes the old path untouched, so clamped input is bit-identical. Elevated output of an unclamped curve is clamped, with the same domain. Closed-form oracle: the uniform cubic span-to-Bezier matrix (one and three spans, the tensor product for a bicubic patch, and `P·Mᵀ` / `M·P` for surfaces clamped in one direction) plus the one-step Bezier elevation formula; rational and multi-span elevations match the input to 1e-12. Eight of the nine regressions fail on `main`; the full `remus-math` suite passes. |
| <a id="b152"></a>B152 | **Curve-fitting entry points took any size and degree (kernel review 2026-10-14, MATH-8)** — `interpolate`, `interpolate_with_params`, `approximate` and `approximate_lspia` accepted any point count and degree. Coincident consecutive points repeat a chord-length parameter, fail the collocation band check and sent `interpolate` to its dense n×n fallback: 4000 points with five duplicates allocated 128 MB before returning `SingularMatrix`. A fitted degree of 26 or 2²⁰+1 points fitted without complaint, and `approximate` on 8192 points with 512 control points formed dense normal equations for 25.7 s (`ci-test` profile). Reached through WASM `interpolatePoints`, `approximateCurve` and `approximateCurveLspia`. | `math/src/nurbs/fitting.rs`, `math/src/lib.rs`, `math/src/diagnostic.rs`, `wasm/src/error.rs`, `math/tests/regress_fitting_bounds.rs` | S | Untrusted input reaches these through the browser kernel; an unbounded dense allocation or an O(m²·n) assembly is a denial of service, and a late `SingularMatrix` hides which point is at fault. Exit: every entry point refuses oversized or degenerate requests with a typed error before assembling any system. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** two additive `MathError` variants with stable codes. `ResourceLimitExceeded` (`resource_limit` / `resource_limit_exceeded`, details `resource`, `limit`, `actual`; WASM `resource_limit_exceeded`) bounds the point count (`MAX_FIT_POINTS` = 2²⁰), the fitted degree (`MAX_FIT_DEGREE` = 25, after the existing cap at the point count, so a large request through few points still fits) and any dense matrix (`MAX_DENSE_FIT_ENTRIES` = 2²², 32 MiB: the interpolation fallback's n² and least-squares' n·(m+6) + 2m², each separately allocated basis row counted at six entries of overhead). `CoincidentFitPoints` (`invalid_input` / `coincident_fit_points`, detail `index`) refuses interpolation when a chord-length parameter does not increase — points that coincide or differ below parameter resolution — before the system is built; a fully coincident set keeps its uniform parameters and still fits. A non-finite chord-length parameter (finite points whose chord lengths overflow) is refused as `ParameterOutOfRange` by interpolation and least squares, as `interpolate_with_params` refuses it. Degree 0 is `InvalidDegree` up front. `perf_n01_duplicate_points_keep_typed_refusal` now pins the earlier typed refusal instead of `SingularMatrix`. Arithmetic is bounded too (`MAX_FIT_WORK` = 2³⁰, resource `fit_work`): least squares' n·m² + m³ (3320 points × 836 control points fits the entry budget but needs 2.9·10⁹) and LSPIA's basis visits n·(p+1)·(3·(max_iterations+1) + 40) (three passes per iterate plus the 20-round step-size power iteration); the in-tree SSI and self-intersection callers (degree ≤ 3, 100 iterations) fit up to 780 000 points per chain. Banded and per-point basis storage is bounded as well (`MAX_SPARSE_FIT_ENTRIES` = 2²⁴, 128 MiB, resource `sparse_fit_entries`): interpolation's band n·(3p+1) — 2²⁰ points at degree 25 would have allocated 608 MiB — and LSPIA's basis table n·(p+1+6), each point's separately allocated row counted at six entries of overhead (10⁶ points at degree 15 held 16·10⁶ values but also a million allocations); a cubic fit through every admitted point stays inside it. LSPIA's O(n·m) nearest-parameter initialization is now an O(m log n) binary search that returns exactly the linear scan's index (first minimizer, rounding ties included), pinned by a differential test. Non-finite or unsorted parameters (finite points whose chord lengths overflow) keep the scan, whose n·m comparisons are charged to `MAX_FIT_WORK` together with the iterations. Not bounded here: `surface_fitting.rs`, whose row fits go through the bounded `interpolate_with_params`. Residual: `approximate` still assembles NᵀN densely; a banded assembly would make it O(n·p²). |
| <a id="b153"></a>B153 | **LSPIA returned its last iterate, unmeasured, as success (kernel review 2026-10-14, MATH-9)** — `approximate_lspia` measured iterates 0 to `max_iterations − 1`, applied one more update and returned that polygon without measuring it, as `Ok` whether or not it had converged (non-convergence was a debug-only `log::warn!`). LSPIA's least-squares residual falls monotonically but its maximum deviation need not: on thirty collinear points with one spike and eight control points it returned 3.613 after sixty updates when 3.560 had been reached after one, and with five control points 3.519 against the initial polygon's 2.585. Reached through SSI chain fitting, self-intersection curves and WASM `approximateCurveLspia`. | `math/src/nurbs/fitting.rs`, `math/src/nurbs/fitting/lspia_oracle_tests.rs`, `math/tests/regress_lspia_best_iterate.rs` | S | A fit that silently returns a worse curve than it found, and calls a missed tolerance success, is undisclosed quality loss (the B21 lesson: a log line is not disclosure). Exit: the best measured iterate comes back with its deviation and a convergence flag. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** `approximate_lspia` returns `LspiaFit { curve, max_error, iteration, converged }` (public Rust API change; the SSI, self-intersection and WASM callers take `.curve`). All `max_iterations + 1` iterates are measured; the first under the tolerance is returned as converged, otherwise the first with the smallest maximum deviation with `converged: false`. The crate-private weighted variant selects the same way. The cached-basis bit-identity oracle keeps the verbatim pre-cache loop and now runs it once per iteration count to recover every iterate, measures each with `evaluate`, and requires the new result to equal the selected one bit for bit (curve, deviation, iteration, flag); every existing oracle case passes. WASM `approximateCurveLspia` still returns only an edge, now the best iterate; it does not expose the flag (a detailed binding or a typed refusal is a public JS API choice left open). Not changed: `surface_fitting::approximate_surface_lspia`, which returns its last iterate the same way. |
| <a id="b154"></a>B154 | **Surface LSPIA returned its last iterate unmeasured (kernel review 2026-10-14, surface twin of MATH-9)** — `approximate_surface_lspia` measured iterates 0..`max_iterations` − 1, applied one more update and returned that grid unmeasured, as `Ok` whether or not it converged. LSPIA's least-squares residual falls monotonically but the maximum deviation need not: on a 7×7 flat grid with the centre raised to z = 1 (degree 1, 3×3 control points) the residual falls 10.18 → 0.856 at every step while the maximum deviation goes 0.889, 0.982, 0.695, 0.921, … and settles at 0.825, so a budget of 1 returned a worse surface than a budget of 0 and every budget ≥ 3 a worse one than iterate 2. Fix (owning layer `math/src/nurbs/surface_fitting.rs`, mirroring curve B153 in #1088): `approximate_surface_lspia` returns `LspiaSurfaceFit { surface, max_error, iteration, converged }`; all `max_iterations + 1` iterates are measured, the first under the tolerance returns as converged, otherwise the first with the smallest maximum deviation with `converged: false`. Callers `operations/src/untrim.rs` and WASM `approximateSurfaceLspia` take `.surface` (JS signature unchanged). | `math/src/nurbs/surface_fitting.rs` (`lspia_surface_deviation_never_grows_with_budget`, `lspia_surface_reports_convergence`), `math/src/nurbs/surface_fitting/lspia_oracle_tests.rs` | S | A larger iteration budget must never return a worse fit, and callers need to know whether the fit met its tolerance. Exit: re-measured deviation non-increasing in the budget at independently computed averaged chord-length parameters; every result bit-identical to the matching iterate of the verbatim old loop. | **Done (2026-10-14, #1092):** the budget regression fails on `main` at `7ad88cc2` (budget 1: 0.982 after 0.889) and passes after. The bit-identity oracle keeps the old loop verbatim, recovers iterate *k* by running it with budget *k* and tolerance 0, and checks iteration, flag, `max_error` and every knot, control point and weight bit for bit over budgets 0–30 on four raised grids (non-monotone, monotone, and one that diverged before B155) and three converging cases; a converged result also equals the old loop's own return. The step-size divergence found while building these cases is [B155](#b155). Once a best iterate exists, an iterate that cannot be built or whose deviation is non-finite ends the run and the best is returned (Codex review on #1092: before B155 a diverging run overflowed near iterate 1,050 and a larger budget turned a valid fit into `InvalidControlPointValue`); with the B155 step no valid input reaches that guard, which stays as defence. |
| <a id="b155"></a>B155 | **Surface LSPIA step size diverged on ordinary grids (found while fixing B154, 2026-10-15)** — `approximate_surface_lspia` stepped by 1 / (u factor × v factor) with each factor the largest column sum of *squared* basis values, i.e. the largest diagonal entry of NᵀN, which is a lower bound on λ_max, not an upper one. LSPIA converges only for steps in (0, 2 / λ_max), so ordinary fits diverged: a 10×10 plane at degree 3 with 6×6 control points went 0.19 → 120 (10 iterations) → 3.5e13 (50) in x/y and overflowed near iterate 1,050; `lspia_surface_fits_plane` passed because z never moves on a z = const plane, and `lspia_surface_fits_paraboloid` passed by comparing z at parameter (0.5, 0.5) with z at position (0.5, 0.5). Reached through `untrim` (grids above 100 points) and WASM `approximateSurfaceLspia`. Fix (owning layer `math/src/nurbs/surface_fitting.rs`, `col_sum_lambda` only): sum the basis values, not their squares. NᵀN has non-negative entries, so its largest row sum bounds λ_max (Gershgorin), and that row sum is the largest column sum of N because each row of N sums to at most 1 (partition of unity); the tensor bound is the product of the two directions. The step 1 / bound sits inside the convergence interval, matching the curve fit's 1 / λ_max. | `math/src/nurbs/surface_fitting.rs` (`lspia_surface_plane_converges_in_every_coordinate`, `lspia_step_bound_dominates_lambda_max`, `lspia_surface_fits_paraboloid`), `operations/src/untrim.rs` (`untrim_lspia_fit_passes_near_its_samples`) | S | A divergent fit silently returned garbage (before B154) or the initial grid (after); every LSPIA surface refit inherited it. Exit: the plane converges in every coordinate with strictly falling deviation; the bound dominates a dense power-iteration λ_max on six knot layouts. | **Done (2026-10-15, #1092):** with the old step restored, the plane, bound and corrected paraboloid tests fail; with the fix all pass. The plane now converges monotonically (0.19 → 1.1e-3 at 50 iterations → 3.9e-6 at 1,000; 1/bound is conservative, so convergence is steady rather than fast). The paraboloid test now checks the surface against z = x² + y² at a 5×5 parameter sample (< 0.02 after 100 iterations). Every probed raised grid (5–9 points per side, degree 1–3, 3–6 control points) has a monotone falling residual. B154's bit-identity oracle shares the step helper with its verbatim reference loop, so it keeps checking selection on the corrected iterates. Untrim's own tests (≤ 100 samples) never reached its LSPIA branch; the new untrim test fits 30² samples of a bicubic patch with 20² control points and measures each sample at its averaged chord-length parameters: 0.134 under the old step (divergent, so B154 fell back to the initial net), under 0.02 now. Trade-off: C is within 1.4–17 % of a dense λ_max on the probed layouts, so the step is about as large as the curve fit's 1 / λ_max. Where the old step happened to converge (as many control points as samples per side), it was about 1.8× faster, because it sat near the 2 / λ_max edge, the same edge it crossed when samples outnumbered control points. At untrim's 50 iterations, a 15² fit reaches 4.1e-3 instead of 2.4e-3, and a 30² fit reaches 6.3e-3 instead of falling back to 0.134. |
| <a id="b156"></a>B156 | **NURBS construction and evaluation panicked on degenerate input (kernel review 2026-10-14, MATH-1/2/5)** — three paths reachable from a STEP file or a WASM call. (2) An end knot repeated more than `degree + 1` times passes construction, and `find_span` returned the empty end span, so evaluation at the domain end divided zero by zero.
