Exit: hostile curve input is a typed error or a finite answer, never a panic. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** `NurbsCurve2D::new` applies the 3D curve's degree, knot and weight checks. `find_span` clamps to the nearest non-empty span at both domain ends (unchanged for clamped knot vectors). Degree elevation uses a checked `u64` binomial and refuses with `InvalidDegree` before decomposition. Four of the five regressions fail before the fix; the in-range elevation control passes on both. |
| <a id="b157"></a>B157 | **2D polygon offset, fillet and clip returned wrong profiles (kernel review 2026-10-14, MATH-6/MATH-7)** — `offset_polygon_2d` intersected adjacent offset lines and cut self-intersections by vertex count, so an offset past a feature's size came back inside out: a 4×2 rectangle inset by 1.5 returned a clockwise loop of area −1, and inset by 2.5 a counter-clockwise loop of area 3 built from four reversed edges. Its miter limit also beveled joins where the offset edges overlap, which put a 0.5 inset of the (0,0) (10,0) (5,20) triangle's apex 0.12 below the source apex (area 84.48 against 76.02). `fillet_polygon_2d` set its tangent points `r` from the corner instead of `r / tan(α/2)` and bent the chord with a parabolic bulge, so a regular octagon of circumradius 10 filleted at 1 reached \|p\| = 10.2346. `sutherland_hodgman_clip` read a clip edge's left as inside and returned [] for a clockwise clip polygon. Reached through WASM `offsetPolygon2d`, `fillet2d` (direct and batch) and `intersectPolygons2d`. | `math/src/polygon_offset.rs`, `math/src/polygon2d.rs`, `wasm/src/bindings/polygon2d.rs` | S | Silent wrong 2D profiles under sketch and profile operations: an inside-out inset, a fillet outside its outline, an empty clip. Exit: closed-form areas for rectangle, chamfered-square, regular-polygon, L, dumbbell and C-shape offsets in both windings; octagon, stadium and L fillets; the clip of overlapping squares in all four windings; property tests against independent oracles. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the miter loop is checked against the region Clipper's raw offset path winds around positively (miter joins where the offset edges diverge, a detour through the source vertex where they overlap): the path is cut at its self-intersections, each piece kept where the winding steps between non-positive and positive across it, and the kept pieces traced. The miter loop comes back unchanged, vertex `i` still the offset of vertex `i`, when it is simple and has the region's area (the detours differ from it by kites that only remove area inward and only add it outward); otherwise the region's boundary, or an empty result when the whole polygon collapses. The miter limit bevels diverging joins only. Fillets use the tangent setback, sample the true arc, split an over-subscribed edge in proportion to its corners' setbacks and shrink their radii to fit. The clip takes its inside side from the clip polygon's winding. All 13 closed-form regressions fail on `main` (`0186a84`); three property tests (convex insets against a half-plane intersection, unbeveled star insets simple and at least `d` inside, the traced region against the raw path's winding number on a grid) pass at 30,000 cases. Full `remus-math`, `remus-operations` and `remus-wasm` nextest 4,681/4,681 (35 skipped). Residuals, not chased: the single-loop result returns only the largest piece of an offset that splits, and only the outer boundary of one that encloses a hole (the C-shape returns 169 for a 160 region; a multi-loop result needs a new API); the pre-existing diverging-join bevel is one vertex at the midpoint of the two offset feet, which for a sharp corner lies well inside `d` of the source vertex (1.03 for `d` = 3.25 at the property test's reflex corner), and a square join would add a vertex and break the vertex correspondence; `offsetPolygon2d` still has no public work budget.  Integration follow-up preserves representable requested fillet gaps even within the relative rounding slack; slack dedup applies only where corners requested the whole edge. |
| <a id="b158"></a>B158 | **Planar pcurve deviation read 0 (kernel review 2026-10-14, HC-6, fail-open)** — `heal::fix::edge::compute_pcurve_deviation` evaluated the surface at each pcurve sample through `FaceSurface::evaluate`, which has no answer for a plane, and skipped the sample, so every planar pcurve measured deviation 0. A pcurve shifted 0.5 off its edge passed `repair_pcurve_within_budget(1e-30)` untouched, and Auto-mode `fix_same_parameter_on_face` never rebuilt one. Fix: evaluate a plane through its canonical chart (origin `n·d/|n|²`, axes from `Frame3::from_normal`), the chart the heal projector writes and the STEP writer and strict same-parameter validator read; a NaN sample now reads as an unbounded deviation instead of being dropped by `max`. The check and its rebuild also follow the use: a reversed use is sampled from the edge's end, and its rebuilt pcurve takes the reversed parameter range (as the topology validator and `builder_solid` read it), so a correct reversed pcurve no longer measures the edge length off. The rebuild of a line or circle edge on a plane writes the exact chart `Line2D`/`Circle2D` (phase and winding included), the only forms strict validation certifies there; a NURBS fit would read as repaired and then fail `check_same_parameter_strict` (`planar_circle_repair_writes_a_certified_chart_circle`). A planar rebuild that still deviates beyond tolerance, because the edge's own curve lies off the plane, restores the original pcurve and refuses with the new `RepairRefusalKind::PcurveDeviationRemains` instead of reporting a repair (`off_plane_edge_keeps_its_pcurve_and_refuses`). A circle within tolerance of its plane keeps the analytic chart circle (`planar_circle_within_tolerance_of_its_plane_stays_analytic`), and `repair_pcurve_within_budget` answers to its own budget, not the context tolerance (`budgeted_planar_repair_answers_to_its_budget`). | `heal/src/fix/edge.rs` (`repair_budget_tests::{plane_pcurve_deviation_is_measured_in_the_canonical_frame, plane_repair_measures_an_offset_pcurve_against_its_budget, auto_same_parameter_rebuilds_only_the_offset_planar_pcurve, reversed_planar_use_is_measured_and_rebuilt_along_the_use}`) | S | A repair budget that cannot see a wrong pcurve certifies it. Exit: a planar pcurve's deviation is its true 3D distance from the edge, and a budget refuses what it cannot meet. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the three regressions fail on `main` at `0186a84` (deviation 0 for a 0.5 offset; the 1e-30 budget accepted) and pass after. Boolean results carry no planar pcurves (probe: 0 of 50 planar uses on a box cut twice), so `fix_shape` on them is unchanged; pcurves stored in a non-canonical frame now read as deviating and Auto mode rebuilds them in the canonical one.  Integration qualification: hard-budget planar fits without an exact line/circle chart reconstruction are refused and restored; fixed samples cannot certify an ellipse between nodes. |
| <a id="b159"></a>B159 | **Wire fixers overwrote the reorder they followed (kernel review 2026-10-14, HC-7)** — in `heal::fix::wire::fix_wire_impl`, `fix_reorder` rebuilt an out-of-order wire and recorded `wire → reordered` in the `ReShape`, but `fix_degenerate` and `fix_tail` then read the ORIGINAL wire, rebuilt it from that stale order, and recorded `wire → rebuilt` over the same key. A triangle stored `[e0, e2, e1]` with a closed zero-length edge, and an open chain with a 1e-9 tail, both came back `[e0, e2, e1]`: the degenerate edge or tail gone, the order lost. Fix: every fixer runs on the wire its predecessors left (`ReShape::current_wire`, the end of the replacement chain) and records `current → rebuilt`, so the replacements compose. | `heal/src/fix/wire.rs` (`degenerate_removal_composes_with_the_reorder`, `tail_removal_composes_with_the_reorder`), `heal/src/reshape.rs` | S | A heal that reports `WireReordered` must not hand back the unordered wire. Exit: a reorder survives every later wire rebuild in the same pass. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** both regressions end `[e0, e2, e1]` on `main` at `0186a84` and `[e0, e1, e2]` after. The B17 qualification matrix pinned the bug: its On-policy wire-order cells refused with `ClosureGapTooLarge` because the gap and closure passes measured the stale unordered wire; they now commit and pass the same repaired-solid checks as Auto (`operations/tests/qualify_heal.rs::b17_wire_order_matrix`).  Integration qualification: a reorder that flips duplicated seam occurrences is refused as `AmbiguousSeamPcurve`; occurrence authority is never guessed from occupied orientation keys. |
| <a id="b160"></a>B160 | **Collinear-vertex collapse unpaired a chord with two interior vertices (kernel review 2026-10-14, HC-2 + HC-10)** — `heal::upgrade::collapse_collinear_vertices` (run on the mesh-fallback boolean path) planned every candidate vertex against the original topology, so on a chord `A–V1–V2–B` shared by two faces it planned V1 → `(A, V2)` and V2 → `(V1, B)` at once; each wire took whichever junction it met first, and the two sides ended on different edges (once-used edges 4 → 8 on a two-face fixture; 6 → 10 on the reported mesh-fallback case). It also iterated std `HashMap`s, so plan order and the reused P→Q edge varied run to run. Fix: one pass takes only plans whose edge pairs are disjoint, in vertex order, and passes repeat until nothing collapses (each productive pass shortens a wire); merged edges created in a pass are shared by `(P, Q)`; maps are `BTreeMap`/`BTreeSet`, and the file leaves the det-hash grandfather manifest. Each merged edge remembers the vertices it absorbed across passes, and extending it again must keep all of them within tolerance of the new chord, so a chain of individually acceptable collapses cannot drift (`repeated_collapses_keep_every_absorbed_vertex_within_tolerance`). | `heal/src/upgrade/collapse_collinear_vertices.rs` (`adjacent_collinear_vertices_collapse_to_one_shared_edge`), `scripts/det-hash-grandfather.txt` | S | A watertightness repair must not open the seam it was meant to tidy. Exit: a collinear run of any length collapses to one edge both faces share, deterministically. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the regression reads 8 once-used edges on `main` at `0186a84` and 4 (unchanged) after, both faces sharing one A–B edge. |
| <a id="b161"></a>B161 | **Heal passes skipped cavity shells and hole loops (kernel review 2026-10-14, HC-3 + HC-5)** — the pipeline builtins `direct_faces`, `same_parameter` and `sew_shells`, `remus_heal::fix::solid`'s coincident-vertex merge, and the operations-layer `merge_coincident_vertices`, `close_wire_gaps` and `remove_degenerate_edges` read only `outer_shell()`, and several of them only each face's outer loop. Still outer-shell-only, not chased here: `operations::heal::{remove_small_faces, remove_duplicate_faces}` and `unify_faces` planning (each needs a per-shell guard, not just a wider walk).  Endpoint repairs copy edges retained by other faces or standalone wires, preserve per-use pcurves and winding, and isolate shared face/shell roots before boundary rebuilds. | <a id="b162"></a>B162 | **Degenerate-edge removal skipped the self-loop vertex merging leaves (kernel review 2026-10-14, HC-9, fails closed)** — `operations::heal_solid` merges vertices (`close_wire_gaps`, then `merge_coincident_vertices`) before `remove_degenerate_edges`, and the merge fuses both ends of a sub-tolerance edge into one vertex. `remove_degenerate_edges` measured length by the endpoint chord and skipped every closed edge (`start == end`) to spare full circles, so the collapsed line stayed in both wires and `heal_solid` refused a unit box with a 1e-9 edge (1 + 2 validator errors). The same chord test would delete a near-full arc whose ends sit 1e-9 apart. Fix: an edge is degenerate when it is shorter than tolerance along its curve: a line by its chord (a fused self-loop is 0), a short-chord curve by sampling its parameter domain, keeping the old endpoint verdict only when the curve has no parameter authority. A closed curve (one vertex) is never degenerate there, as before: a rim bounding its cap alone keeps it, so dropping it from the lateral wire would unpair it. An edge is removed only when an upper bound on its length is below tolerance: circle, parabola and hyperbola arcs by arc length, an ellipse arc by semi-major × span, a polynomial NURBS by its control polygon; a rational NURBS is kept (`degenerate_edge_test_never_undershoots_the_length`). | `operations/src/heal.rs` (`edge_is_degenerate`), `operations/src/heal/tests.rs` (`heal_removes_a_short_edge_its_vertex_merge_collapses`, `degenerate_edge_test_measures_along_the_curve`, `rims_shorter_than_tolerance_stay_in_every_wire`, `degenerate_edge_test_never_undershoots_the_length`) | S | Healing a sliver edge must not refuse the solid it was asked to repair. Exit: a box with a sub-tolerance edge heals to the clean box; full and near-full circles survive. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the box regression is refused on `main` at `0186a84` and heals after (4-edge faces, volume 1 within 1e-9). The pass order is unchanged: with the length measured along the curve, removal handles the fused edge wherever the merge leaves it. |

With positive weights (enforced by `NurbsSurface::new`) the convex-hull property makes that a guaranteed bound. `face_aabb` itself, and so `properties::bounding_box`, is unchanged: on a rational NURBS the net bulges past the surface (the extruded-ellipse box test pins it exact), and the sampled box still under-bounds a narrow bump there (not fixed here). | <a id="b164"></a>B164 | **Line/NURBS intersection missed a narrow bump's far-flank crossing (found while fixing HC-8, the NURBS face box)** — `intersect_line_nurbs` seeded Newton only from an n×n grid. On a degree-2×2, 30×30 surface over the unit square (flat at z = 1, control point (13, 13) raised to 1.5, a bump about 3/28 wide with its peak 1.28125 at (12.5/28, 12.5/28)), the classifier's first base ray from (peak, peak, 1.1) found only the two crossings behind its origin (t ≈ −0.285, −0.031): no 20×20 grid node passed the distance test on the far flank, and the flank candidates that did slid off the bump to the behind-origin roots. On the cube's top face `classify_point` then counted the wrong parity and returned Outside for a point inside. | `math/src/nurbs/intersection/{line.rs,line/subdivision.rs,line/bump_oracle_tests.rs}`, `check/src/classify/boundary/nurbs_bump_ray_tests.rs`, `check/tests/regress_nurbs_bump_ray_crossing.rs`, `math/benches/intersection_inner_loops.rs` | S | Silent misclassification of points near steep NURBS features; the fixed seed grid gives no completeness guarantee. Exit: every transversal crossing of a line with a NURBS surface is reported, against an independent oracle, without changing the roots the grid already finds. | **Done in source (2026-10-15, this PR):** a completion pass after the grid isolates crossings by convex-hull bounds of the two plane distances through the line (a span-block AABB tree built once per surface, then per-span Bézier extraction and de Casteljau subdivision to nearly affine boxes) and runs Newton clamped to each surviving box, accepting a root only when both plane distances from the line are within the rounding their own arithmetic can commit (256 ulps per axis of the surface's and the rebased line origin's coordinates, weighted by each plane normal; no size term, so a large, elongated or translated surface, or a large coordinate along the line, cannot widen it), with a nonsingular interval Jacobian proving each leaf holds at most one root, and weights divided by each block's and span's largest so neither a common nor a regional weight factor can overflow the values or shrink them into the rounding slack. Grid roots keep their bits and order (the pre-O06 bit-identity oracles still pass); missed roots are appended. Regressions fail before the fix: the exact repro and a 168-ray narrow-bump sweep against a graph-bisection oracle (math), and the three classifier base rays (check). Cost (criterion, `nurbs_ray_*`): a line missing the surface's control box adds ~50 ns; on a 120×120 bicubic a hitting ray goes from 9.4 to ~28 µs and the per-surface build from 78 to ~150–170 µs, which the one-shot `classify_point` path pays per ray (`PreparedSolid` pays it once); small-surface hits rise 10–30%; the narrow-bump ray goes from 12 to 33 µs and gains its missing root. Combined qualification: the control-hull face bound is integrated and `regress_nurbs_bump_ray_crossing` is enabled.  Integration follow-up: the end-to-end bump witness is enabled together with conservative face bounds; uncertain NURBS tangent rays abstain and use classifier recovery while math contacts remain available. |
| <a id="b165"></a>B165 | **Classifier recovery rays were unreachable (kernel review 2026-10-14, HC-11)** — `check::classify::classify_point_with_source` cast three base rays with binary in/out votes and exited as soon as two agreed, so one side always reached two and the recovery loop behind them, with `ClassifyOptions::max_recovery_attempts`, never ran. Nothing could tell a bad ray from a good one either: an interior point of a tetrahedron whose vertices sit on two of the base rays counted 0 crossings on both (each ray meets three faces exactly at their shared corner, and every face's trim test rejects it) and read Outside against the clean third ray's 1. Fix: a boundary skeleton (every edge of the classified faces: a line as its segment, a curved edge as a 32-chord polyline sampled the first time a ray passes through the edge's certified `remus_geometry::bounds` box, with a closed-form plane-and-radius test ruling rays out of circle edges first; boxes padded by a margin of 1e-9 of the extent or 256 ULPs of the largest coordinate, whichever is larger, so a solid placed far from the world origin still catches its rounded vertex rays (`rays_through_vertices_abstain_far_from_the_origin`), widened per query for an origin farther out, boxes included (`rays_from_a_distant_origin_graze_thin_edge_boxes`); the ray-segment distance projects across the ray, so a nearly parallel crossing segment still reads 0; under a BVH above 16 edges) lets a ray passing that close to an edge or vertex abstain; recovery directions stand in until two clean rays agree, then a lone clean vote, then the old base-ray majority. With no ray grazing, the vote is unchanged. One-shot, `PreparedSolid` and `ClassificationCache` (bytes accounted) share the skeleton. | `check/src/classify/graze.rs`, `check/src/classify/mod.rs` (`rays_through_vertices_abstain_and_recovery_decides`, `skeleton_flags_exactly_the_rays_through_vertices`, `skeleton_flags_rays_through_curved_edges`, `grazing_rays_draw_recovery_directions_up_to_the_option`), `check/src/classify/graze.rs` (`circle_prefilter_keeps_every_ray_near_the_circle`), `check/src/classify/{prepared.rs,cache.rs}` | S | A ray through a vertex is the classic ray-casting failure, and the option meant to absorb it did nothing. Exit: a ray through the boundary skeleton never votes while a clean one is available, and the option bounds how many replacements are drawn. | **Done (2026-10-14, this PR):** the tetrahedron regression reads Outside on `main` at `0186a84` and Inside after (one-shot, prepared, cached); a sweep of 216 such tetrahedra (vertex distances 0.5–3 along each ray, six origins) read 61 Outside on `main` and none after. Not covered: a hit near a curved edge farther from its chords than the margin (the chord sag, as in the trim polygons), and tangent grazes on curved faces. Cost: one graze test per counted ray (`classify_perf`, noisy shared host, against `main`): one-shot +15–29% box, +20% cylinder and sphere; prepared +50% box (12 box tests per ray against a sub-microsecond query), +38% cylinder, sphere unchanged.  Closed-curve skeleton chords follow the classifier trim polygon's projected seam, including wrapped NURBS parameters; a shifted-circle-seam regression pins a ray far from both endpoint balls. |
| <a id="b166"></a>B166 | **The fixed Gauss rule straddled knots on many-span NURBS faces** — `integrate_parametric` tiled each axis of a NURBS face into at most `MAX_PATCHES = 16` uniform patches regardless of the carrier's knots (only the adaptive path read them), so on any carrier with more spans than that nearly every Gauss rule crossed a continuity break and the result stopped converging with the order: a 35 × 55-span bicubic face read 2140.7138 / 2140.9695 / 2140.9116 at orders 8 / 12 / 16 against a converged 2140.957231134 (~1e-4), and `mass_properties`, `integrate_face`, `integrate_face_area` and the validator's orientation probe all run that rule at default options. Fix (owning layer `check` only): the fixed rule keeps every cell inside one knot span whenever the face's window holds an interior knot (`integrate_knot_aligned`): `u` breaks at the trim's vertices, the interior `u` knots and every abscissa where a trim segment crosses an interior `v` knot (`UvTrim::knot_crossing_breaks`; without it real faces stall near 1e-7), each abscissa's `v` spans cut at the interior `v` knots, cells at most the old tiling's patch, so a span is never cut finer than the old density and an axis holds at most its span count plus 16 cells, work capped at 2¹⁸ cells with a typed `IntegrationFailed` past it. A window with no interior knot keeps the old rule bit for bit; the adaptive path is unchanged. **Decision: default, not opt-in** — the aligned rule is the correct answer at equal cost, and every bit-identity contract compares two readings of the same rule (area-only vs full, cache vs fresh, volume-only vs full), so they hold. | `check/src/properties/face_integrator.rs`, regression `crates/check/tests/regress_nurbs_knot_aligned_quadrature.rs`, unit tests `knot_crossing_breaks_land_where_segments_cross_v_knots`, `knot_aligned_rule_defers_without_interior_knots_and_caps_its_work` | S | Default mass properties, areas and volumes of every imported multi-span NURBS face were 1e-5–1e-4 off whatever order was asked for. The B28 probe pin `crates/io/tests/b28_probe_memo_identity.rs` re-recorded the hammer holder's signed volume: 50 245.521 289 691 05 (2.2e-7 off converged adaptive, order 8, eps 1e-10) → 50 245.532 161 524 716 (4e-17 off it); the three other fixtures did not move. Residual: trimmed faces stay limited by the trim outline's chording, as before; a carrier needing more than 2¹⁸ aligned cells (e.g. > 512 × 512 spans untrimmed) now refuses where the old rule returned a 1e-4 answer. |
| <a id="b261"></a>B261 | **Exact box − pointed-cone cuts kept the wrong cone band** — a 2×3×4 box cut by a radius-0.5, height-4 cone with its base at z = 2 returned `Exact` volume 24 − π/24 instead of 24 − 0.875·π/3; apex-inside cuts refused. The periodic splitter now accepts a single rim and an edgeless apex level. | <a id="b262"></a>B262 | **Involute gears (consumer: OpenZCAD gear feature)** — `operations::gear::make_gear` builds an external spur gear, an internal (ring) gear or a straight rack as one planar outline extruded once, with no boolean: planar caps, `Cylinder` tip/root lands and root fillets, and ruled-NURBS involute flanks (a rack is planes and cylinders only). Each flank is a C¹ cubic B-spline from the new `math::nurbs::hermite_fit`, whose deviation from the true involute is bounded in closed form (`‖P⁗‖ = r_b·√(9 + t²)`), so the result discloses `quality: "approximate"` with a proven `flankDeviationBound` (default 1e-5·module). ISO 21771 dimensions, profile shift, backlash and undercut / thin-tip / thin-rim warnings; unbuildable profiles refuse transactionally with typed `gear:<reason>` codes that carry the dimensions. Exposed as `makeGearDetailed` (direct and batch). Tier: core (creates live solid handles in the session). `operations::gear::cut_gear_teeth` / `cutGearTeethDetailed` cut teeth along the whole length of an existing boss (external) or bore (internal), keeping its diameter (module solved) or turning it to a given module: an exact intersect first proves the rebuilt tooth band is solid material (a cross hole refuses as `gear:features-in-tooth-region`), then a coaxial tube cut and a gear-ring fuse meet on one coaxial seam, so no flank ever meets a cylinder. Every gear face carries a construction-derived role (`tooth[k].flank.plus`, `root[k]`, `cap.start`, …) for consumer naming, carried through the fuse where it is one-to-one. Helical gears (`helix_angle`, `hand`; ISO 21771 normal module, pressure angle and coefficients) turn the transverse profile along the axis through a twisted extrusion (`extrude::twist`): lands concentric with the axis stay exact cylinders while they sweep less than a full turn (past that a cylinder face would cover itself twice, so they are swept too), and flanks, fillets and helix edges are certified screw-motion fits (`math::nurbs::screw`), with `flankDeviationBound` covering the exact involute helicoid; they also cut into a boss or bore. Herringbone gears (`herringbone`) reverse the hand at the mid-plane: two twisted halves that share their mid-plane edges form one solid with no boolean, and each half's swept faces carry a `.start-half` / `.end-half` role. Straight bevel gears (`make_bevel_gear` / `makeBevelGearDetailed`): the pitch angle follows from both tooth counts and the shaft angle, flanks are cones over the spherical involute (a certified Hermite fit on the sphere, centrally projected exactly onto the flat heel plane), and the outline sweeps toward the apex through a tapered extrusion (`extrude::taper`) whose lands are exact cones. Cylindrical involute (ZI) worms (`make_worm` / `makeWormDetailed`): the worm is the involute helical gear with its starts as teeth and helix angle 90° − γ, built through the same twisted extrusion over many turns, with axial-module conventions (ISO/TR 10828); `worm_wheel` / `makeWormWheelDetailed` give the meshing wheel, the helical gear of the worm's hand with helix angle γ and transverse module `m_x`, and the center distance. Throated wheels (`make_throated_worm_wheel`, `throated: true`): the tips follow the torus about the worm axis that keeps its root clearance across the throat and stand at the outside diameter either side, built without a boolean (each tip land split into two cylinder strips and an exact torus patch; each flank trimmed along a certified Hermite fit of its torus intersection, whose fourth-derivative bound is carried through Faà di Bruno); the exact GFA cut of the helical wheel by the torus is `ExactOnlyUnattainable`. Double-enveloping (Hindley) worms (`make_enveloping_worm` / `makeEnvelopingWormDetailed`): the hourglass blank less the wheel's straight-sided teeth swept with it, built without a boolean: lands and root floors are exact torus patches between rails that are lines in the torus's own parameters, flanks are ruled patches between certified rail fits, every patch cut to under half a turn (a NURBS face winding several turns leaves trim projection seeded on the wrong turn), and the end planes meet the tori in exact circles and the flanks along certified section fits. Double-enveloping wheels (`make_enveloping_worm_wheel` / `makeEnvelopingWormWheelDetailed`): exactly the Hindley straight-sided tooth in the mid-plane; off it each flank stays straight in every section, its turn and offset quadratic in the height and fitted inside the tooth a hob of the worm's shape cuts (the hob's positions all pass through the mid-plane line with different tangent planes, so the hobbed tooth and the wheel's flanks crease along it, each flank two ruled faces); the fit is sampled against the hob, not proven, and its largest sampled `relief` is reported; tips follow the throat torus and the outside cylinder beside it, built without a boolean. Not covered: a wheel flank proven inside the hobbed tooth between samples, hobbed (enveloping) flanks on a cylindrical worm's wheel (a throated wheel keeps the crossed helical flanks and their point contact), ZA/ZN/ZK thread forms, thread run-outs (the end caps cut the thread square), back and front cones at the heel and toe (they are planes, so the tooth ends run out flat), spiral and Zerol bevels, crown gears (face angle over 80° refuses), and the trochoid root a generating cutter leaves (the root is a tangent circular fillet), helical racks, a relief groove between herringbone halves, and teeth over part of a face (`gear:partial-span-unsupported`: the exact boolean refuses a turned groove whose wall passes the boss, refuses splitting a cap whose inner loop is a tooth outline, and coincident coaxial walls merge only with aligned seams). | `math/src/nurbs/hermite_fit.rs`, `math/src/nurbs/screw.rs`, `operations/src/gear.rs`, `operations/src/gear/profile.rs`, `operations/src/gear/cylinder.rs`, `operations/src/extrude/twist.rs`, `operations/src/gear/bevel.rs`, `operations/src/gear/worm.rs`, `operations/src/gear/throat.rs`, `operations/src/gear/enveloping.rs`, `operations/src/gear/hindley_wheel.rs`, `operations/src/gear/jet.rs`, `operations/src/extrude/taper.rs`, `wasm/src/bindings/gear.rs` | M | First kernel-owned standard-part generator; the certified Hermite and screw fits are reusable for other closed-form curves and for the 7.1 twisted sweep. | **In review (2026-10-29):** closed-form dimension, exact-involute Green-area volume, pitch-circle thickness classification, fit-bound, rack trapezoid-volume, placement, determinism and refusal tests; direct/batch WASM parity. |

## Performance work packages

All 111 packages from the 2026-09-12 audit are retained, with their original
priorities, phases, dependency IDs, acceptance criteria and pinned source references.
Read evidence and experiment safeguards in the [performance audit](../performance/kernel-optimization-roadmap.md).
`PERF-` is the namespace on this page; dependencies use the original short IDs.

One **owner** is assigned per package. Update both the package disposition and its
owner's remaining scope here when a slice lands; completing a child does not close
its parent. `Proposed` is an audit proposal, not a claim that existing mechanisms
are absent. M01/M06/M10 retain broader scope beyond O3.1a; N04 retains scratch and
consumer-adoption work beyond #411. Baseline-era measurements are not current-head
benchmarks. Priorities are within the performance lane, subordinate to current
correctness and consumer needs.

The CSV/JSON are generated from the tables below with
`python3 scripts/sync-roadmap-inventory.py`; verify with `--check`. Do not edit
those exports by hand. They contain work specifications, not implementation status.

**Evidence:** M = measured at the audit baseline; S = source inspection;
H = hypothesis/design. None implies a shipped speedup. **Priority:** P0 = evidence,
P1 = first optimizations, P2 = selected by profiles, P3 = experiment.
**Effort:** S/M/L/XL ≈ 1–3 days / 1–2 weeks / 2–6 weeks / multi-month work per
qualified slice, not a delivery date. Calibrate estimates after profiling.

### Performance phases


The program has nine phases. The phase number is an implementation lane, not a requirement to finish every item in an earlier lane before starting an independent later one. Use the explicit dependency map in the CSV/JSON to select ready work. Every optimization PR should be bounded to one mechanism and one measured workload family.

| Phase                                 | Deliverable                                                                                                            | Scope and dependency                                                                                                                                                                        | Exit gate                                                                                                                                              |
| ------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **0 — Establish evidence**            | Reproducible baseline dashboard, missing workloads, phase/copy/allocation counters and honest gates                    | M01–M08, M10, S01, W01, W06. Extend existing O3.1 rather than restarting it.                                                                                                                | Pin every artifact; report quality, latency and memory; identify top functions and growth terms on procedural and imported workloads.                  |
| **1 — Remove local structural costs** | Small, independently reviewable optimization PRs                                                                       | Line projection, chaining, compact interpolation storage, derivative outputs, scratch reuse, sketch allocations and binding/render copies. Each needs its own baseline/correctness fixture. | Before/after improves the targeted metric outside noise; useful full-workload effect or a justified scaling/memory win; no new correctness regression. |
| **2 — Prepare and index**             | Reusable query context, conservative bounds, invalidation substrate and sparse candidate generation                    | Q01→Q02; Q05 before curved pruning; B02–B06, healing/sewing indexes, common validation/recognition preparation. Can proceed alongside Phase 1.                                              | Repeated queries avoid rebuilding; sparse candidate growth drops; mutate/restore and unknown-bound tests pass.                                         |
| **3 — Local transactions and memory** | Transaction coordinator, chosen undo/COW design, shared immutable payloads, staged imports, later versioned compaction | T01→T02/T03; T04/T05; W04 and I02. Compaction T06 follows explicit handle/checkpoint design and lifetime measurements.                                                                      | A fixed local edit no longer scales with unrelated document size except justified bookkeeping; rollback and handle safety hold under injected failure. |
| **4 — Incremental derived work**      | Mesh, edge sample and property reuse; changed-region recomputation; efficient export/render consumers                  | Q02 and cache correctness; D03 before D04; V03 can reuse transaction boundaries.                                                                                                            | Mutate-then-recompute matches a cold full recomputation, including neighboring boundary dependencies; bounded memory with predictable eviction.        |
| **5 — Subsystem specialization**      | Profile-selected NURBS/SSI, sketch, blend/offset, modeling, IO and render improvements                                 | Select by measured frequency × cost and capability needs. Each row is a separate work package; this phase is not one giant rewrite.                                                         | Per-family correctness oracles, real-model benefit and distribution behavior are demonstrated; unsupported cases stay explicit.                        |
| **6 — Deterministic concurrency**     | Tuned native scheduling, more tessellation/integration/GFA parallelism, optional WASM threads                          | Pure task interfaces and boundary ownership first; aggregate thread-local counters. WASM deployment requirements follow separately.                                                         | 1/2/4/8/16-thread data, identical deterministic contracts, cancellation, small-input crossover and peak-memory budgets.                                |
| **7 — Hardware/compiler experiments** | SIMD, specialized layouts, GPU crossover, PGO, allocator/build variants and optional transport formats                 | Only after a representative baseline identifies the relevant cost. Keep portable and general fallback paths.                                                                                | Held-out workloads improve; native/WASM quality and artifact-size/load budgets pass; close no-gain experiments without shipping them.                  |
| **8 — Qualification and publication** | Combined-kernel regression budgets and reproducible comparison results                                                 | Repeat after each phase, not only once at the end. Real competitor runners remain distinct from the synthetic scorecard.                                                                    | Exact-source native/direct/batch WASM plus real-model, memory/lifetime, failure, determinism and compatibility gates pass.                             |

The shortest path to useful improvements is **0 → selected Phase 1 items**, while cache and transaction designs proceed independently. Incremental work depends on invalidation; broad parallelism depends on task isolation; all phases feed qualification. Do not wait for arena compaction before fixing a sampled line projection, nor attempt persistent caches before their mutation behavior is defined.

### Spatial bounds, classification and distance

Inspected entry points: [source 1](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/blob/ec42cea4dbceed980957feba2979426854e4eb96/crates/math/src/bvh.rs#L212), [source 2](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/blob/ec42cea4dbceed980957feba2979426854e4eb96/crates/check/src/classify/mod.rs#L311), [source 3](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/blob/ec42cea4dbceed980957feba2979426854e4eb96/crates/check/src/distance/mod.rs#L50), [source 4](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/blob/ec42cea4dbceed980957feba2979426854e4eb96/crates/operations/src/distance.rs#L57), [source 5](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/blob/ec42cea4dbceed980957feba2979426854e4eb96/crates/algo/src/classifier/ray_cast.rs#L159).

| ID | Priority / phase / evidence | Work | Measurement and acceptance | Effort / risk | Dependencies | Owner | State |

| <a id="perf-q01"></a>PERF-Q01 | P1 / 2 / S | Build one operation-local prepared query context holding face IDs, conservative bounds, BVH, analytic descriptors and trim data; reuse across rays and points. | Compare 1/100/10000 points on one solid, including recovery rays; prove equivalence to existing paths for cavities, seams and OnBoundary cases. | M/medium | M04, M07 | [O3.2](#o-o3-2) | Done (2026-09-26, [#704](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/704)): `check/src/classify/prepared.rs::PreparedSolid<'a>` borrows the topology (lifetime bars stale reuse — no global cache, revision scheme or journal hook), holding the face list, filtered conservative bounds with one shared BVH, and per-face trim polygons with an on-demand fallback preserving the original typed errors. Both paths run the same `ClassifySource` vote loop, so ray ordering, recovery, tolerance handling and determinism match by construction; `RobustClassifier`'s winding fallback and a new `classify_points` batch use it. Measured on unit cube (profiling profile): 10000 points one-shot 19998 BVH builds / 119988 bound evals / 9522 trim builds in 105.7ms vs prepared 1 / 6 / 6 in 4.8ms; criterion release box-10000 12.3x, cylinder-1000 1.8x, sphere-300 2.2x, single query including preparation still wins on box. Differential equality to one-shot on a fixed Halton corpus plus analytic truth for box, hollow (inner shells), cylinder seams, sphere caps, torus band, NURBS box/cylinder, translations, scales 1e-3/1/1e3 and near-boundary probes. Scope note: surface carriers stay live reads of the borrowed topology (NURBS is borrowed, never cloned; only small analytic descriptors copy per ray), so no descriptor cache was needed; the 3-way-disagreement recovery loop and the sphere 2-plane patch branch share the dispatch and inherit exactly but had no direct trigger in the corpus. O3.2's mutation-invalidated persistent cache remains pending (see PERF-Q02). |
| <a id="perf-q02"></a>PERF-Q02 | P1 / 2 / S | Add persistent spatial caching after mutation invalidation is proven: topology incarnation plus mutation generation first, finer per-entity revisions later. | Mutate-then-query, raw mutable access, tolerance changes, failed edits, checkpoint restore and same-ID/different-topology cases never reuse stale entries; bound cache memory. | L/high | Q01, M05 | [O3.2](#o-o3-2) | Partial (2026-09-30, [#880](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/880)): whole-topology cache identity in `remus-topology` (`CacheIdentity` lineage plus monotonic mutation generation; restores bump instead of rewinding, overflow poisons) and a bounded persistent classification cache in `remus-check` (`classify/cache.rs`, FIFO capacity 16 via `with_capacity`) built on the PERF-Q01 `PreparedSolid` substrate, with per-kernel session integration in direct plus batch `classifyPoint`. Every mutating route bumps generation before a mutable reference escapes (audit in [contract](../design/perf-q02-cache-identity.md) §2); tolerance and `max_recovery` are cache keys, malformed handles never cache. Criterion `classify_cache_perf` box-10: repeated 10000 one-shot 46.7 ms vs persistent 8.48 ms per 2x10000 (5.5x including rebuild); occasional 10x100 plus unrelated alloc 9.4x; constant-edit near parity with no speedup claimed; thrashing 32 solids over cap 16 still correct with zero hits. Retained 1,824 B per entry. Invalidation proofs: 14 check plus 4 topology plus 2 io round-trip plus 2 wasm tests vs one-shot, prepared and analytic truth. Declared bounds: whole-topology invalidation only (any allocation drops all preparations); classification only (distance reuse unchanged); different tolerances duplicate preparation; per-face tracking, automatic mesh invalidation, per-face property caching and arena compaction not attempted. |

Remaining: the math-layer `Bvh::query_closest` internal traversal stack still allocates per call (the `into` variants reuse the candidate buffers, not that stack); a shipped BVH traversal for check distance remains declined on measurement. |

Spatial culling of the face descriptors, and cylinder and cone faces, remain |

The [2026-10-13 refresh](../performance/face-drag-refresh-2026-10-13.md) selects the fine curved-face quantity stage for finer attribution and qualified acceleration. Consumer-visible since 2026.1.28 (#922): the grouped stream is a pure function of the geometry — `fuse_solids([plate, wall])`, the same fuse on operand copies, and `boolean_with_entity_evolution` on copies tessellate byte-identically at 0.008/0.06 where `4fdccf80` gave three different streams (repeat tessellation of one handle was byte-identical at both). OpenZCAD's union display-cache test used stream identity as kernel-call identity and double-counted its evolution probe's copy; OpenZCAD#522 counts calls per solid handle and deflection instead. |

Triangulations bit-identical (differential tests against the legacy predicates in `cdt/tests/predicate_oracle.rs`); mesh box −57%, 64-hole plate tessellation −40%. Walk, flip, Steiner and fallback-scan instrumentation at 1k/10k/100k points remains |

### Validation, mass properties and measurement

Inspected entry points: [source 1](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/blob/ec42cea4dbceed980957feba2979426854e4eb96/crates/check/src/properties/face_integrator.rs#L97), [source 2](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/blob/ec42cea4dbceed980957feba2979426854e4eb96/crates/check/src/properties/mod.rs#L149), [source 3](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/blob/ec42cea4dbceed980957feba2979426854e4eb96/crates/check/src/validate/mod.rs#L50), [source 4](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/blob/ec42cea4dbceed980957feba2979426854e4eb96/crates/operations/src/measure/volume.rs#L2916), [source 5](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/blob/ec42cea4dbceed980957feba2979426854e4eb96/crates/io/benches/nurbs_properties.rs#L24).

| ID | Priority / phase / evidence | Work | Measurement and acceptance | Effort / risk | Dependencies | Owner | State |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="perf-v01"></a>PERF-V01 | P1 / 2 / M | Prepare trim loops, projected boundaries and quadrature/evaluator data once per face per operation, then reuse across volume/area/inertia/validation. | Measure the hammer-holder benchmark and analytic/cavity fixtures; preserve periodic UV branches, trim orientation and compensated/local-origin arithmetic. | M/high | M04, M07 | [B28](#b28) | Proposed |
| <a id="perf-v02"></a>PERF-V02 | P1 / 4 / S | Cache face property contributions and recombine only changed faces using construction history. | Repeated measurements become proportional to changes; compare against full recomputation after every supported edit/undo, including inner shells and face reversals. | L/high | Q02, V01 | [O3.2](#o-o3-2) | In progress — bounded exact-content face-integral reuse, volume-only orientation requests, whole-volume and standalone NURBS-area memos already ship; finite-reference guards and independent oracles are retained. [Matched first post-edit quantity measurement](../performance/perf-d01-boundary-clearance.md) reduces warmed-process WASM volume 492/496 → 254 ms through D01 boundary-distance pruning, preserving fresh volume bits and native Float64 quantity mesh fields at the unchanged effective deflection. Recognition remains ~347 ms; profile its graph area/relation work next. The volume clamp (`diag × 5e-5`) now rounds down onto the grid `{1, 1.5} × 2^e` (`measure::volume::quantize_down`), so an edit that moves the bounding box by a few percent keeps the effective deflection and its untouched faces keep their cached meshes: on the hammer holder's wall moves (native, WASM caches on) the post-edit volume drops 351–368 → 156–175 ms, the cold first reading rises 460 → 440–530 ms at the finer 3.906e-3 clamp (was 5.397e-3), and the reading moves 0.86 toward the exact integral. Volume values change by that finer clamp. S1: a body sent to the closed whole-solid mesh is read off its fixed-order face integrals instead (`measure::volume::qualified_face_integral_volume`), the strict validator's own requests through the face-integral cache, when four certificates hold: no face is in a class the integrator mis-measures, every shell's vector areas cancel to 3e-5 of its area, every edge lies on the faces it bounds to 1e-4 of the body's extent (sampled at up to the integrator's own 128 points per edge), and each face's order-5 term agrees with an independent order-3 one (summed face by face to 1e-6 of the volume). The qualification corpus that ruled out an uncertified route (a periodic NURBS wall read at half, a sphere cap 15× over, zeroed NURBS bands, NURBS-holed planes, parabolic bands bounded by chords, a variable fillet off its edges) all declines to the mesh. On the hammer holder the reading moves to 50245.22, the fine-mesh limit (clamp mesh 50241.7 or 50242.6 on the grid; it read 50245.53 while planar faces took their free-form NURBS edges as a 32-chord outline, a sliver the closure certificate cannot see because an extrude's two caps miss it alike, which left OpenZCAD's spline extrudes up to 7.9e-4 of their volume short; unit-weight polynomial edges of degree at most six now integrate exactly, span by span; unsupported rational and higher-degree boundaries retain the previous fallback), its post-edit volume drops 156–225 → 12–15 ms (native, WASM caches on), and the first reading after the import's validation takes 271 ms (the order-3 terms are new work). A warm reading may differ from a cold one by rounding (within 1e-12, as the validator's probe); it is no longer bit-identical. General history-driven contribution recombination remains open; source-plus-swept-prism seeding stays declined. |
| <a id="perf-v03"></a>PERF-V03 | P1 / 3 / S | Deduplicate repeated validation of the same immutable result within a transaction, using an explicit validation certificate keyed to all relevant policies. | Instrument each validator invocation; preserve full required checks at trust boundaries and invalidate certificates on any change or rollback. | L/high | T01, Q02, M04 | [B28](#b28) | Proposed |
| <a id="perf-v04"></a>PERF-V04 | P2 / 2 / S | Share topological indexes among edge-use, shell, Euler, orientation and finite-geometry checks rather than rebuilding traversals per check. | Measure allocations and entity visits on large valid and malformed bodies; identical diagnostic severities, entity IDs and acceptance are required. | M/medium | Q01, M04 | [O3.2](#o-o3-2) | Proposed |
| <a id="perf-v05"></a>PERF-V05 | P2 / 5 / H | Specialize additional qualified analytic property integrals and reuse shared moment evaluation across requested outputs. | Closed-form independent oracles, thin walls, trimmed holes and translated models pass; do not infer full primitive volume from its carrier alone. | M/high | M07 | [B20](#b20) | Partial — area slice (2026-10-19): cylinder, cone, sphere and torus face areas reduce to exact boundary line integrals of the face's own edge curves (Green's theorem; `face_integrator::analytic_face_area`, its own face-cache family), falling back bit for bit to the order-8 trimmed quadrature outside the qualified family. All 66 analytic faces of the hammer holder reduce (per-frame `faceArea` sweep 17.8 → ~4.6 ms native in the drag loop); closed forms (bores, cones, caps, cavities, fillet bands/octants, torus rim bands, a cross-drilled wall) match to 1e-11, the quadrature to its trim chording (≤2.5e-5). Volume terms, centroids and inertia remain on the quadrature. |
| <a id="perf-v06"></a>PERF-V06 | P2 / 6 / H | Parallelize independent face integrations and validation work using deterministic reduction and stable diagnostics. | Compare p95 and reduction error across thread counts, especially cavity cancellation and large translations; retain serial thresholds. | M/high | V01, P01 | [B28](#b28) | Proposed |
| <a id="perf-v07"></a>PERF-V07 | P2 / 5 / S | Accelerate hidden-line/visibility and repeated measurement requests with prepared classification and batched query APIs. | Measure sample-point count, context rebuilds and end-to-end drawing time; preserve silhouette/visibility semantics and declared approximation. | M/high | Q01 | [P-Class 7.5](#p-7-5) | Proposed |

Position-level at tol 1e-7 from base `57888080`: 100k sparse 5.0B checks/43s → 0/89ms, clustered 1.67B/14.5s → 66,666/70ms, chain 2.5B/21.7s → 100k/66ms, dense-coincident 100k checks 0.9ms → 38ms (bounded). Topology-level: 9k sparse 299ms → 5.1ms, 9k clustered 609ms → 3.9ms, 300-vert 0.34ms → 0.12ms (no small-model overhead), dense 3k 0.47ms → 1.1ms (bounded). B17 `qualify_heal` 10/10 green. Limits: dense all-coincident pays bounded linear index overhead; collection scope unchanged (outer shell; operations wrapper outer wires only); degenerate inputs fall back to exact all-pairs. |

Profiling (`--profile profiling`, tol 1e-6, disjoint quads): sparse 40/200/400/800/1600 free edges, theory pairs 780/19.9k/79.8k/319.6k/1.28M, endpoint checks 0, runtime 0.018/0.279/1.066/4.219/17.655ms → 0.050/0.256/0.456/0.974/1.862ms (9.5x at 1600); dense-coincident 800 edges 0.157ms → 0.214ms (bounded); index memory linear (~44KB at 400 edges, ~175KB at 1600). Curve-pruning slice (2026-09-30, bounded analytic midpoint descriptor, [#899](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/899)): certified domain Line plus Circle plus Ellipse only, midpoint at frac 0.5 bit-identical to `curves_agree` k equals 4 sample with same greater-than tolerance, orientation-invariant (1 minus 0.5 equals 0.5) covering forward and reverse with forward precedence preserved, only rejects never accepts, representation-equivalence preserving (different variants sharing same midpoint within tol never pruned), uncertified NurbsCurve plus Parabola plus Hyperbola stay on exact path. Declined as unprovable: curve-type equality (near-straight arc compatible with chord), radius center plane equality (rotated u-axis, coterminal trims 0-pi vs 2pi-3pi, circular ellipse), sampled or endpoint-only bounding boxes (not conservative certificates). Decisions bit-identical vs reference including ambiguity and ordering with pruned plus exact accounting for every reference curve check (15 new unit tests: reversed arc uses, closed rims different radii, major minor arcs, chord arc prune accounting, coterminal trims compatible, circular ellipse compatible, distinct ellipses, ambiguity with distractors, negative large arcs, tolerance-boundary arcs, nontransitive midpoints, uncertified parabolas exact-path, dense-incompatible 100 arcs, dense-compatible 50 pairs, distractor end-to-end with coedge pcurve attributes history idempotence rollback; total 45 sewing tests green). Profiling (`--profile profiling`, tol 1e-6): dense-incompatible 100 distinct arcs sharing 0-0-0 to 1-0-0, theory 4950, ref curve 4950 plus pcurve 0 plus plans 0 plus declined 99, idx curve 0 plus pruned 4950 plus descriptor 4950 plus pcurve 0, wall planning 462us → 337us (27 percent, cheap examinations remain quadratic by design); dense-compatible 50 twin pairs (100 edges), ref curve 2500 → idx curve 50 plus pruned 2450 plus pcurve 50 plus plans 50; sparse 400 lines endpoint 0 curve 0 descriptor 0 pruned 0 (no overhead). Benchmarks (`heal/benches/sewing.rs`, criterion sew_shell wall): sparse 10 50 100 quads 60 287 565us linear, clustered 10 50 100 69 372 833us linear with merges, dense_incompatible 10 25 50 71 225 662us (quadratic cheap, zero expensive), dense_compatible 10 50 100 84 652 2081us (quadratic cheap, linear expensive for twins). Dense legitimate with agreeing midpoints requiring full 7-sample confirmation may remain quadratic for cheap examinations by design, expensive as needed. Remaining fallbacks: NurbsCurve Parabola Hyperbola uncertified exact-path, general finite-span bounding still out. Owned: `heal/src/upgrade/shell_sewing.rs`, `heal/benches/sewing.rs`, `heal/Cargo.toml`, roadmap PERF-H02. No change to extrusion history, topology storage, GFA, measurement, tessellation. Same campaign extends the proven domain: perforated planar faces (bijective hole correspondence), Line+Circle boundaries (frame-invariant sweeps, phase-invariant rims, chord≠arc), collinear/same-circle subdivision normalization, and the attribute-compatibility veto — each with positive/negative fixtures plus verified end-to-end repair (dual validation, census, closed-form volume, ray-cast probes, idempotence, rollback). Same-host sequential evidence in [sketch-perf-s02](../performance/sketch-perf-s02.md): independent 1000-param solves 469–1407x faster with exact rank/DOF/classification agreement (iterations shift honestly, e.g. 11 vs 8), coupled chain/drag controls unchanged, 10000 params solve in ~41 ms where dense refuses, peak RSS 21.7 → 3.2 MiB, packaged-WASM fingerprint matches native. Remaining: one large connected component keeps the dense cubic (S04, now composable per block); per-block trust regions shift iteration counts slightly; the shared runner's dense-Jacobian budget still refuses the 10000 rows (harness follow-up); cross-call plan caching explicitly declined (S05) |

Same-host paired evidence in [sketch-perf-s04](../performance/sketch-perf-s04.md): chain-1000 solve 3.67 s → 3.4 ms (1076x) and detailed 4.45 s → 3.9 ms (1156x) with identical 5/5 iters and rank/DOF/classification; grid-300 6.5x, linkage-300 76x; tangent/ellipse (scalar mixing, bw 199/183), underconstrained (m<n), far closures and below-threshold deficient fall back safely with preserved rollback/nonfinite/fixed/gauge behavior; packaged-WASM `2026.1.22` fingerprint matches native including batch. Remaining: column-reordered (RCM) scalar-mixed coverage, `dof`/multi-block sparse-rank sharing (single-block sparse rank done), and cross-call reuse explicitly declined (S05) |

Separate counters qualify 1,538 rays/14,549 candidate-face calls and 40/42 NURBS-area memo hits per edit. Affected-neighborhood planning, copied-surface minimization and the per-frame remove/rebuild prefix of prepared blend moves remain open. |

#### PERF-O06-NR: bounded NURBS ray evaluation reuse

Locally qualified — [64-entry exact-parameter reuse](../performance/perf-o06-nurbs-ray-reuse.md),
seed-point carry and unchanged ray-norm reuse preserve complete root bits and
shipping geometry, quantities, feature records and edge verdicts. Quiet fresh
WASM recognition falls about 11.0% on the maintained holder edit; kernel plus
bulk-transfer time falls about 5.4–5.5%. All four native carrier controls improve
7.6–11.6%. Both schedules retain 12 edits per revision. Qualification: 8,078
workspace tests, docs, 13 complexity guards, no-I/O contracts, 52-row census,
fresh and installed paired WASM, 36 exact snapshot pairs/180 arena-display
artifacts and 5,760 per-face area-bit pairs. This closes the bounded reuse
tranche locally; hosted CI, committed-package refresh, broader workloads and
browser presentation remain separate.

**2026-10-28 follow-through:** [full-holder evidence](../performance/store-local-holder-rebuild-2026-10-28.md) records a byte-qualified baseline profile and rejected cache/arithmetic experiments. The ray solver and FMA shim remain byte-identical to the baseline. Torus sampling reuse and final source-matched package measurements are reported separately; full consumer timing and stage budgets remain open.

</details>

#### PERF-O06-TR: torus ray norm comparisons

Locally qualified — [finite-component bounds and convergence norm skips](../performance/perf-o06-torus-ray-crossings.md)
retain every quartic iterate, complete root bits, FMA, tolerance and trim
operation. Quiet fresh WASM recognition falls 3.6–3.8% on the maintained holder
edit; kernel plus bulk-transfer time falls 1.6–1.8%. Native 64-ray controls
improve 14.5–33.4%. Each schedule retains 12 edits per revision. Qualification:
8,078 workspace tests, docs, 13 complexity guards, no-I/O contracts, 52-row census,
fresh and installed paired WASM, 36 exact snapshot pairs/180 artifacts and 5,760
area-bit pairs. The preexisting curved-boundary classifier repro stays open;
certified trim-support rejection, hosted CI, package refresh, broader workloads
and browser presentation remain separate.

#### PERF-O06-CM: crossing counts reused across edits

Locally qualified — `operations/src/query/crossing_memo.rs` remembers each
non-planar face's ray crossing count under the face's complete content (the
interned words of `measure::face_content_words`, compared exactly) and the
ray's origin and direction bits; `PreparedSolid::with_crossing_memo` adds a
remembered count in place of counting. Edge relations and recognition after an
edit then recount only the faces the edit changed. Off in the library; the WASM
kernel enables it per thread (65,536 counts, 4,096 face contents, 8 MiB of
content words). Tier: core (per-session kernel state; it reads live face
handles). Raw kernel growth +14 KiB.

On the holder's −6 mm wall offset in the OpenZCAD harness (local overlay, one
core): commit `solidEdgeRelations` 275–338 → 55–63 ms, `recognizeFeatures`
321–325 → 64–74 ms, commit wall 1,291–1,396 → 801–886 ms. The cold import
pays 5 ms per call to intern face contents; its analysis stays within run-to-run
noise (823–994 ms against 873 ms). Natively (`profiling`) the moved body's
relations go 110 → 52 ms and recognition 134 → 77 ms. Relations and recognition
output are byte-identical with the memo off, cold and carried across two face
moves and a cut (`io/tests/hammer_crossing_memo_oracle.rs`) and under eviction
(`query/crossing_memo.rs` tests). Commit volume is unchanged: the move changes
the bounding box, so the measurement deflection clamp re-meshes every face
(owner decision D1).

| <a id="perf-r01"></a>PERF-R01 | P2 / 5 / S | Add a reusable offscreen render session/context; the convenience path currently creates a GPU context per render. | Separate cold device/pipeline setup from repeated-frame cost; preserve device-loss recovery, options and software-adapter behavior. | M/medium | M07 | [B14](#b14) | Done (2026-09-27, [#731](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/731)): `render/src/session.rs::OffscreenSession` owns 1 device + 2 pipeline variants (edges on/off) + 1 size's cached targets; per frame it tessellates, uploads fresh geometry, draws via shared `encode_scene`, and reads back. Convenience path preserved verbatim. Measured dev-profile RTX 3090 Vulkan: cold `Session::new` ~215ms, first render ~33ms, 5x warm avg ~7.8ms/frame vs convenience avg ~220ms/frame (renderer reuse only, not a kernel speedup). Bit-exact vs convenience; edge toggle keeps ids with 549 differing pixels; iso box shows 3 front faces; +1e6 RTC render bit-identical; background/size/solid/doc switches leak-free; validation errors recover, injected loss fails closed via new `RenderError::DeviceLost` (no physical device-loss recovery claimed). R02 geometry cache, async readback, compute-mesher and viewer changes remain open. |

| <a id="perf-r03"></a>PERF-R03 | P2 / 1 / S | Replace per-triangle vertex expansion with per-face indexed render vertices where normals/attributes permit. | Measure GPU bytes and vertex processing; preserve sharp normals and the correct face ID at every triangle and seam. | M/medium | M05, M07 | [B14](#b14) | Partial (2026-09-29, [#877](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/877), integrating #855): per-face indexed render vertices in `render/src/mesh.rs` where normals permit — vertices share within a face on exact RTC position plus normal bits, never across faces (a shared vertex would need two face ids) and never across incompatible attributes. Box 12 tris 36 to 24 verts (33 percent vertex, 29 percent mesh bytes); cylinder 176 tris 528 to 180 verts at deflection 0.02 (66 percent vertex, 58 percent mesh bytes). Image/picking parity via the session/prepared/async suites (bit-exact, edge-toggle ids identical, RTC plus 1e6 bit-identical). Renderer overhead only, not a kernel speedup; committed WASM packages unchanged. Remainder: sharp normals stay split, seams (face boundaries) never weld, edge overlay untouched. |
| <a id="perf-r04"></a>PERF-R04 | P2 / 5 / S | Make readback asynchronous or optional for repeated rendering; separate color and ID readback by caller need. | Measure GPU/CPU synchronization and tail latency; do not reuse buffers before completion or return stale picking data. | M/medium | R01, M07 | [B14](#b14) | Partial (2026-09-29, [#877](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/877), integrating #856): asynchronous selective readback in `render/src/async_frame.rs` and `session.rs` — submit returns a `FrameTicket` immediately, completion via non-blocking `poll_frame` or blocking `wait_frame`, output selection (`Color`, `FaceIds`, `Both`, `None`; unrequested outputs skip copy, map and decode entirely). At most 8 pending frames plus a 512 MiB logical in-flight byte budget; over-budget submit fails with the recoverable `AsyncBudgetExceeded` before allocation. Tests `render/tests/async_readback.rs` (7) and `prepared_render.rs` (10). Sustained 8x512 Both: submit 5–6 ms total, drain 6.7–7.4 ms/frame vs sync prepared 7.1–8.2 ms/frame; decode dominates. Renderer overhead only, not a kernel speedup; committed WASM packages unchanged. Remainder: `wait_frame` waits on the most recent submission, so out-of-order collection also waits for later frames (correct by exclusive per-frame resources); `draw_prepared` keeps synchronous readback (see the `PreparedDrawTimings` note in `render/src/prepared.rs`); discrete-GPU qualification only (software backend unavailable; one sandboxed `offscreen_render` SIGSEGV in the host Vulkan driver passed on isolated retry). |

| <a id="perf-p03"></a>PERF-P03 | P3 / 7 / S | Benchmark release LTO/codegen-unit choices, WASM -Oz versus speed-oriented optimization, and CPU-specific builds where distribution permits. | Track execution, cold load, compile time and artifact size together; retain portable builds and exact shipped-artifact correctness tests. | M/medium | M07, W06 | [B30](#b30) | Proposed |
| <a id="perf-p04"></a>PERF-P04 | P3 / 7 / H | Run a profile-guided optimization experiment trained on a representative corpus, with disjoint holdout models. | Accept only broad held-out gains with no meaningful regressions; record training provenance and repeat after important compiler/algorithm changes. | L/medium | M07, P03 | [B30](#b30) | Proposed |
| <a id="perf-p05"></a>PERF-P05 | P3 / 7 / H | Test allocator choice, compact collection layouts, hot/cold data splitting and assembly-level tuning only after allocation/cache profiles justify them. | Measure total workload cost and memory, not isolated allocation speed; document dependencies, unsafe requirements and native/WASM differences. | L/high | M05, M07 | [B30](#b30) | Proposed |
| <a id="perf-p06"></a>PERF-P06 | P1 / 8 / H | Qualify the combined optimized kernel after each wave and freeze reproducible performance/quality baselines for release. | Native plus actual direct/batch WASM, real models, resource exhaustion, deterministic threading, long sessions and exact-source CI all pass; attach no-gain closures too. | L/high | M03, M05, M07, W01 | [P-Class 8.2](#p-8-2) | Proposed |

</details>

## Decisions and exclusions

| Decision / boundary | Owner and disposition |
| --- | --- |
| Stable C ABI | O4.5 decision record before implementation. |
| External references and partial loading | O5.5 decides kernel versus application responsibility. |
| WASM threads / isolation headers | P-Class 8.7; measured feasibility and explicit consumer deployment decision. |
| Arena compaction / slot reuse | P-Class 8.6 and its versioned checkpoint/handle design; current retirement is not reclamation. |
| First crates.io/npm publish and PyPI publish | O4.2c / O4.3c. Build and dry-run readiness are separate from permission to publish. |
| Playground hosting and second-consumer outreach | O6.2 / O6.3 retain explicit hosting/outreach decisions. |
| Multi-body mesh reader API | B31 compatibility decision before changing return types. |
| v1 fillet API retirement | Maintainer product decision, not required by B23's shared cascade or H5's geometry gate. |
| IGES growth | Declined by default: STEP is the exchange path; IGES remains a declared lossy preview. [O5.7](#o-o5-7) now owns evidence gathering and a narrowly scoped decision for OpenZCAD I06; this planning entry does not reopen runtime implementation or promote IGES to exact exchange. |
| Native CAD and kernel exchange formats | [O5.6](#o-o5-6) owns the design/qualification boundary for XT/XB, SAT/SAB, 3DM and externally translated native formats. Keep optional translator execution/licensing separate from the default Apache-2.0 kernel; no native application writer or source-history reconstruction claim. |
| Shared-face non-manifold topology | Later RFC under RFC 0005; its ordering against O7 requires a decision. |
| Hybrid mesh/B-Rep | O7 design only under the body-model gate. Existing mesh fallback does not grant hybrid-modeling capability. |
| Application history and parametrics | Consumer scope; kernel work covers identity/evolution, not the feature tree or UI. |
| Monolithic mechanical-feature operators / tessellated STEP | Not queued without consumer/corpus evidence; existing composable primitives remain the scope. |
| Retired harness/lattice scenarios | Reopen only with an in-repository consumer fixture. |
| General duplicate-edge merge key | Do not retry the disproven universal key; use the splitter-side construction identified by the roadmap skill. |
| Periodic pinch / general arrangement / coordinated scoop split | Terminal without the named missing primitive in the roadmap skill. Closed bounded box/sphere and torus/box cells do not qualify the general arrangement problem. |
| Mesh co-refinement optimization | PERF-B12 is evidence-gated; it remains unselected until a live disclosed-approximation workload justifies it. |
| Fork lineage | Upstream v3+ behavior requires independent implementation or an explicit Apache-2.0 grant. |
| 2D drawing formats (DXF, DWG, SVG, PDF) and drawing sheets | Consumer scope (OpenZCAD `io-dxf` and its drawings rows); the kernel exposes `section` (cross-section face handles whose boundary wires carry the section geometry) and `projectEdges` (projected visible/hidden polylines), and 7.5 owns silhouettes. Reopen only with a second consumer. |
| Datum geometry, keep-originals, expression units, reference images, workspace conventions | Consumer scope, recorded by the 2026-09-26 consumer overlay so they are not re-asked of the kernel. |
| WASM module tier for new capabilities | Owner rule (2026-10-13, [PERF-W06](#perf-w06)): every new capability declares its tier, `core`, `lazy:<module>` or `native`, in its row or design doc before implementation. `core` (the eager `remus-wasm` kernel) is the exception and needs a reason: the capability edits the live session (handles, journal/evolution, persistent naming, checkpoints, transactions). A `lazy:<module>` capability ships in its own package reached through exact arena documents, like `remus-wasm-io`; planned modules are `remus-wasm-sketch` (GCS solver only; sketch state stays in core) and `remus-wasm-repair` (import repair, read-only analysis). Consumers preload lazy modules at idle so first use is not slower, and no size change may make a workload slower. A kernel growth above 64 KiB raw carries a `WASM-Size:` line in the PR description naming the capability and its tier. Plan: [#1026](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/1026). |

## Session workflow

1. Refresh `origin/main`; inspect live open PR heads and changed files. Select
   **one** owner row and, where useful, one PERF child package. Read its linked
   specification and the roadmap skill's chase filters/terminal cases.
2. Confirm prerequisites and write the bounded acceptance criteria into the PR.
   Use an isolated branch/worktree. A Pending row may already have an open PR.
   A new capability records its WASM module tier first
   (`Tier: core | lazy:<module> | native`; see [decisions](#decisions-and-exclusions)).
3. Verify with independent oracles and the repository checks appropriate to the
   change. Geometry changes retain native coverage, the WASM coverage their tier
   requires (direct and batch for `core`, the module package's own bindings for
   `lazy:<module>`, none for `native`), transactional refusal, scale, census and
   regression requirements from the existing specifications.
   A documentation edit does not establish new geometry or benchmark evidence.
4. Record state and remaining scope, with the exact PR/artifact evidence, as a
   [fragment](roadmap-evidence/README.md) for the owner row, not by editing the
   row: `python3 scripts/roadmap-evidence.py --fold` applies fragments in a
   standalone `docs(roadmap): fold evidence` PR. Edit rows here directly only
   to add or restructure them.
   If blocked, record the blocker in the selected row and stop; do not switch
   items. Update capability/stability claims only when their promotion gates pass.
5. If a PERF work specification changes, regenerate its CSV/JSON exports and run
   `python3 scripts/sync-roadmap-inventory.py --check`. Keep performance evidence
   fixed to its recorded SHA; new measurements get a new evidence directory.
6. Refresh overlap before delivery. Port concurrent changes from retired ledger
   paths to the owner row here. Deliver a ready PR with exact-head check status;
   publishing/deployment and merge remain separate authorized actions.
